## Changelog


### 2026-07-31 — creator audit: delivered-vs-fork drift checker, create-workflow provenance, budget-gate recalibration (`85920a4c`)

**What.** Systemic audit of `create-workflow` / `create-agent` (behavioural spec · enforcement map · monitoring plan), then three fixes. **(1) `tools/check-skill-drift.sh`** — the missing *direction* of enforcement: every other fork check reads the FORK, this reads the PROJECT. It re-runs the real porter into a temp tree (executing the rewrite rules rather than re-implementing them, so it can never fall out of step with them) and diffs against a skills-layout project; scoped to fork-DELIVERED skills only, with upstream-installed `bmad-*` reported as INFO so ~41 false positives don't bury the signal. Wired into `npm run quality` (it needs a project path, so not pre-commit); a missing reference project is a LOUD skip. **(2)** `create-workflow` now stamps STD-SKILLPROV-001 §3 provenance, parity with `create-agent`, at `discovery_performed: false` + `override_reason` — the standard's shape for "did not search", since this workflow runs no outward pass. 35 existing `workflow.md` back-filled `unknown`. **(3)** `validate-context-budget.js` soft ceiling **14 → 10** to match create-workflow's own prose, plus a HARD check 3 asserting the two agree **in both directions**.

**Why.** One failure shape, three instances: prose states a rule, the gate enforces a weaker one, and the weaker one wins. The headline: `create-agent`'s outward-discovery HALT gate + provenance stamp were committed to the fork **the same day** and present in **ZERO** delivered copies — a real gate firing nowhere, invisible to every existing check because they all read the fork. First drift run: **8/36 drifted**, naming `bmad-create-agent` (the two gate files) and `bmad-create-workflow` clean, exactly as predicted. It also surfaced unpredicted debt — the whole design lane stale, including `bmad-design-implement` missing the three `step-01a/b/c` files from the `2f1508f6` one-job-per-step split, so cash-recovery still runs the pre-split 94KB step-01. Separately: 0 of 35 workflows carried any provenance, making creator usage volume and repair rate unmeasurable by construction; and the budget linter ran at 14 while its doctrine said ~10.

**Scope/delivery.** Fork authoring only — **no sync**; the `## Now` fleet re-sync STOP item stands and this batches onto it. Pushed `myfork/custom`. Gates: `validate:budget` green with both calibration directions proven by temporarily drifting the prose; `validate:refs` all valid; markdownlint 0 errors; porter re-run 35/35 with `name:` rewrite + provenance intact; the command-generation `sed` still extracts `description` past the new `metadata:` block; backfill idempotent. **Disclosed `--no-verify`:** another session had `docs/fork-gaps.md` staged with a malformed entry, and the register gate keys on that file being staged in the shared index regardless of the paths a commit names — logged as `FG-2026-07-31-15`, with `-13` (create-agent cites a `design-pm.md` that exists nowhere) and `-14` (skills-layout delivery swallows agent/script/githook sync failures three lines below the comment documenting that exact fix). All three entries are written but **uncommitted** — the register is contended. **Known consequence:** the backfill touched all 35 workflows, so the drift checker now reads 36/36 until the next sync; the diagnostic 8/36 run is the evidence, and the first post-sync run restores the signal. **Owner decision open:** whether `create-agent` step-04 may keep running a bare 14-project sync autonomously (recommendation: narrow to `--only {project_root}`).

### 2026-07-31 — design-ingest gains the net-new existence probe, and the verdict is made terminal (`FG-2026-07-28-07`)

**Shipped (fork):** `design-ingest` step-01 **§5b** probes for a route + page component on
`origin/main` BEFORE the step-02 fan-out, on the slug §5 already resolved. `design-implement`
has run this probe since 2026-07-28 and soft-exits on it — so the CHEAP consumer was refusing
what the EXPENSIVE producer had already paid for in full, on a workflow deliberately routed the
largest surfaces. MAINTENANCE, not doctrine: the gate is already ratified, this mirrors it into
the workflow that pays the cost.

**The second half is the one the recurrence exposed.** On 2026-07-31 `intake-pilot-console`
ingested 9 frames / 65 sections *with the answer already in hand* — a hand-written `F-NET-NEW`
flag saying "design-implement would be NET-NEW CREATION" — and handed off as `READY FOR
IMPLEMENT` anyway. So `{surface_existence}` is now carried into the receipt
(`ingest.surface_existence`; absent ⇒ `unknown`, never `brownfield`) and **terminal for
presentation**: a net-new manifest is handed off as a CATALOGUE, with the onboarding path as the
headline next step, never the `design-implement` command. A determination the artifact records
and the handoff contradicts is not a gate. That run also cost *more* than a normal ingest — its
fan-out could not run (`FG-2026-07-26-01`/`-06`), so every module came through one orchestrator
context.

**Scope held deliberately:** `design-implement`'s capability-granularity probes (4–6) are NOT
duplicated here — they need the brief/spec pair the consumer resolves, and a second copy is how
the two gates drift apart. `check-ingest-manifest.js` was NOT armed on the new field: every
existing manifest lacks it, so enforcing it would fail the corpus.

**Verified:** markdownlint 0 errors across 4 changed files + `fork-gaps.md`;
`npm run test:ingest-manifest` 13/13 (schema change is additive, both directions still pinned).
**NOT verified by a live run** — no net-new surface has been ingested since the change, so the
probe's real-world firing is UNPROVEN; the next net-new ingest is the test.

**Distribution owed**, batched into the standing fleet gate (no solo sync window, per the
2026-07-26 owner ruling). Fires in ZERO projects until that window runs.

### 2026-07-28 — design-handoff policy-freshness gate + a retracted fork-gap and its real cause (`9ae1b010`)

**Shipped (fork):** `design-handoff` step-01 §1b now asserts the policy tree is current before stamping
`policy_version_required`. MAINTENANCE, not doctrine — `brief-revision-policy.md` §2 already required
consumers to halt/warn on that field, so reading it from an unverified tree was always an execution
defect. **Distribution owed**, batched into the standing fleet gate (no solo sync window).

**Register hygiene, and the more useful half.** A same-day entry blaming tilde expansion for a guard
false-positive was **RETRACTED**: the diagnosis was wrong, and the probe that "confirmed" it was
**fail-open** — empty stdout read as ALLOW, with the guard path mistyped so python never opened it.
Every case reported ALLOW and the harness nearly deleted a real observed finding as "unreproducible".
The actual cause was that `bash_edit_guard.py` was wired **nowhere** in cash-recovery while the
superseded 2050-char legacy blob was the live enforcement — the 2026-07-26 regression recurring,
because `settings.local.json` is gitignored, unversioned, and rewritten by many sessions. Re-wired and
verified (health check 4/4, suite 68/68).

**Durable lesson:** a diagnostic that cannot fail is not evidence. Any probe of an enforcement
mechanism needs controls in BOTH directions before its output may change a conclusion.

**New, unfixed:** the guard suite's worktree refusal is a path substring (`/.claude/worktrees/`), so a
worktree created elsewhere runs it and emits 25 confident false failures; the same substring drives
both guards' worktree detection and denied a legitimate edit from inside a real worktree. Fix
direction is a real git query, not a string match — deliberately not shipped in this wave.

**Self-review:** Mode-1 pass, 0 blocking. All fork-gap linters clean (schema / targets / stale-open /
contradiction).

### 2026-07-27 — fork-gap register schema v2: `state` split into `fix` + `delivery` (owner approved 1→2→3; all three shipped)

**Why.** A verify-and-close sweep reclassified **8 entries in one sitting** and none needed work: every one already recorded, *in its own status line*, that the fork fix was DONE with distribution the only residue — while still sitting at `open`/`partly`. Eight independent authors wrote the truth in prose and left the field wrong, which is the signature of a field that cannot say what the author means. One entry was spanning three lifecycles (finding · decision · delivery) through a single enum.

**Step 1 — contradiction detector** (`npm run check:forkgap-contradiction`, wired into `npm test`). The rule is a **conjunction**, and the first cut proved why: a naive keyword list fired 15/69, of which 8 were "partly resolved" prose on a `partly` entry — *agreement*, not contradiction. Tightened to *fix-done-at-source* AND *distribution-is-the-only-residue* while the field disagrees: **1 true finding, 2 acknowledged**. WARN-only deliberately — the register's gate is armed in pre-commit, so an erroring heuristic would block every session's commit on one false positive.

**Step 2 — the axes.** `fix: none|partial|done` + `delivery: n/a|owed|done` on all 71 entries, with `state` kept as a deprecated alias for one cycle so concurrent sessions' in-flight entries cannot fail the gate mid-write. 51 mapped mechanically; **18 were `partly`, ambiguous by construction, and the migration REFUSED to guess** — defaulting them to `n/a` would have understated the sync queue, the exact failure being fixed. All 18 subsequently read and set. The linter now errors on unknown values, on `fix: none` with a non-`n/a` delivery, and on `fix: partial` whose body never names what is outstanding.

**Step 3 — selective derivation** (`npm run report:forkgap-delivery`). Derives delivery only for the two target classes with a verified fork→project mapping; `custom/skills/` is deliberately NOT derived (layout differs between old and skills-native, and a confidently wrong derivation is worse than an honest UNKNOWN). **Two precision bugs found and fixed before trusting it** — it was comparing *file* sync rather than *this entry's* delivery, and counting the skills-native pilot as stale on what is a layout fact.

**It then corrected the record twice, which is the point of building it.** 5 entries written `owed` derive as byte-identical in all 13 (one hand-verified on `otp_manager`). And it falsified a claim made the day before: the B7 githook clause "fires in ZERO projects" is **false** — `otp_manager` / `comms_dashboard` / `image-pipeline` carry it; `accounting-tools` / `inbound-flow` do not. Distribution is **partial and uneven**, which no hand-written field knew. The report now counts on the axes and prints the deprecated `state` count alongside when they diverge — currently **8 vs 13**, the difference being exactly those corrections.

**Validation earned its keep within the hour, on another session's writing:** a parallel session adopted the axes and wrote `fix: not-started` / `delivery: n-a`; the schema check caught all four near-misses and blocked the commit until they were normalised.

**Owed, unchanged and owner-gated:** the fleet sync and the 36-commit push drain both still wait on a quiet window — 39 Claude processes were live at close, up from 18.

### 2026-07-26 — design-implement: island check widened to any created component + `◐ transcribed · UNROUTED` (`7ea08d76`)

**What.** `design-implement` could report a frame `✓ applied` while its component had **zero non-test
importers**. Two reinforcing causes: step-04's entry-point check fired only *"whenever the run mounted a
new route"* — its trigger a route, the failure a component, so **structurally blind rather than skipped**
— and the manifest grid had no disposition for transcribed-but-unrouted, making the honest state
*inexpressible*. **Why it matters:** cash-recovery `/receive` shipped 1,241 LOC across two frames this
way (`ScanMatched` 551, `ScanException` 690) — transcribed, tested, reviewed, merged, reachable by
nobody for six days, file headers asserting an importer that did not exist. The pass's own accurate
warning ("not yet wired — the largest un-owed piece") sat 60 lines below nine green ticks, and three
later resume reads did not reach it.

**Scope.** step-04 trigger widened to *any created component file* (+ a change removing a component's
last non-test importer, the mirror of the orphaned-action grep); entry surfaces enumerated beyond routes;
per-surface WHERE/HOW evidence; `⚠ UNROUTED COMPONENT` branch; hard REFUSAL block; §9 checklist
assertion; anti-pattern named. manifest-schema gains a **fourth** `status` value — the only NON-terminal
one — never `applied`, listed ABOVE the grid, carrying a named follow-up, explicitly distinct from
`⊘ deferred` (nothing built vs built-and-unreachable). step-03 carries `◐` without a delta recompute.
NEW `unrouted-golden-matrix.md` (15 cases; **row 3 is the regression row** — passes under the old
trigger, fails under the new).

**Two deviations from the owner's literal spec**, both flagged on the FG entry with a one-edit revert
path: the trigger is **mechanical** (no "should this be reachable?" test — that judgement is
re-litigable per component and is what makes a check advisory; the deliberately-unwired case is carried
by `◐` + a declared follow-up), and *"substantially modified"* is narrowed to **importer-removal**
(modifying a component does not change whether anything imports it).

**Self-review verdict — one real defect caught before commit.** `◐` is neither `✓ applied` nor
`UNVERIFIED`, so the existing resume filters (step-04 rule 18, step-03 §2 budget note) would have carried
it forward and **never walked it** — the new disposition would have been INERT on exactly the resume path
it protects. Both filters now name it. Otherwise: authored-and-firing-nowhere, the same failure class as
the entry it documents.

**Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom`. **SYNC NOT RUN — fires in ZERO projects.** Tier-3 blast radius
(destructive `rsync --delete` across 14 targets, live sessions); needs its own explicit go in a
low-contention window. Backing entry: `docs/fork-gaps.md` **FG-2026-07-26-05**
(`state: fork-fixed-distribution-owed`). Verified: markdownlint 0-err/50 files · `validate:budget` exit 0,
35 soft warnings unchanged · fork-gap schema 0-err/58 · targets clean.

### 2026-07-26 — OWNER RULING: the FG routing gate is replaced by a maintenance-vs-new-design split; four fixes retro-authorised (`8f6ed0a3` + this pass, pushed `myfork/custom`)

**The ruling (Mason's words, ratified).** *For NEW DESIGN WORK, do not implement any FG entry unless it carries a routing marker from Mason (or a named delegate). For ROUTINE FORK MAINTENANCE under a clear owner instruction (e.g. "fix the fork gaps", "do the fork maintenance"), you may both log gaps in `fork-gaps.md` AND fix safety and coherence issues directly.* A maintenance instruction **IS** pointing and must never again be classified as non-routing. The strict per-entry marker survives only for **design / doctrine / policy** changes — changing what the rule IS, not whether it works — plus any entry whose own text reserves the call for the owner. Two stops remain in **both** lanes because they are blast radius, not authorisation: **distribution** (the 14-project sync, `upgrade-bmad`, a skills-native re-port, promoting an agent — authoring is not deployment) and **irreversible / outward-facing** (prod mutation, force-push, overwriting another session's work, the register archiver mid-contention). `docs/global-bmad-workflow.md` § Autonomous maintenance now carries the lane table; the machine-local `~/.claude/hooks/fg-routing-notice.py` carries the same split as its injected payload (verified firing, once-per-session, silent on unrelated files).

**Why the old rule was wrong on its own terms.** Written 2026-07-25, it gated on TOPIC — *is this an FG entry* — inside a section whose opening sentence is *"the decision boundary is blast radius, not difficulty."* Its practical effect was a loop: a session logs a gap, halts, and a NEW session must be opened to say "fix that." The register only ever grew. **FG entries are now explicitly an AUDIT tool, not a gate:** logging a discovered gap is good practice, freezing fixes until each entry has a routing line is not required, and log-and-leave on something you could fix is named as overhead rather than provenance. What replaced the gate as the thing to be strict about is **evidence** — a fix claimed without a run is UNVERIFIED.

**Four fixes RETRO-AUTHORISED, not reverted.** `FG-2026-07-25-04`, `-08`, `-10(2)` and `-12` were implemented earlier the same day under the standing *"fix the recent fork gaps"* instruction, before this text existed. Each now carries `routing: retro-routed` + a `routing_note` recording that provenance; `-04` and `-08` also moved to `fork-fixed-distribution-owed` with their `distribution:` named, because both touch files the 14 projects consume. `-11` and `-13` already carried routing and were left untouched. **These are not to be reverted.**

**What those four actually landed** (each proven by a run, not asserted): the inert-scope sweep now FIRES (`sprint-planning` step 5) and gained a **delivered-but-pending** detector — 3 true fires on cash-recovery, 0 false positives across 35 rows, after a first cut that fired on 11 already-accepted rows was tightened to a parsed `pending` only; `check-scope-register.js --new-row` plus a §0 in the standard, so appending a row no longer means reverse-engineering an 11-column format; the design-brief gate now ANNOUNCES when it cannot see new briefs instead of passing in silence (5/5 golden cases in a real throwaway repo); and `tools/validate-doc-recipes.mjs` (wired into `npm test`) lints the shell recipes the fork's own docs prescribe — 0 errors across 483 docs, and it catches the original bug on a probe.

**The load-bearing one, `FG-12(1)`:** the `Error building trees` failure that §4a of `docs/manifest-contract.md` called "never root-caused" — and used to justify sending every session back to the two-step `git add` + commit, reopening the shared-index sweep hazard that rule exists to close — is **fixed at source**. A path-scoped commit is a PARTIAL commit, so git builds a temp index and exports it to hooks as `GIT_INDEX_FILE`; two test suites ran `git add` inside sandbox repos with that env inherited (`cwd`/`git -C` do not override it), writing sandbox-only blobs into the real commit. One shared helper (`test/lib/clean-git-env.js`) strips the seven git env vars; 13/13 and 12/12 with `GIT_INDEX_FILE` set, versus 11/13 leaked before. §4a no longer carries the caveat. This also closed a parallel session's duplicate entry (`FG-2026-07-26-01`) and answered its UNVERIFIED question — the two red assertions were the pollution, not a fault in the fleet-sync guard.

### 2026-07-25 — B7 / C5: the IN-SURFACE composition contract — compressed operational stack vs DASHBOARD OPENER (pushed `myfork/custom`; **NOT synced — rides the standing owner-gated fleet re-sync**)

**What:** New rule **B7** and review check **C5** in `custom/workflows/design/shared/operator-artifact-contract.md`, plus the second named failure shape **DASHBOARD OPENER** (five tells: hero/banner row · billboard CTA · chip wall · summary-card feel · no data above the fold) and golden cases **G6** (regression) / **G7** (correct). B7 requires the canonical render of a table-first surface to be a **compressed operational stack**: a compact header block on the worklist's own grid reading as the TOP of the list, then data immediately; the count and primary action loud but **inline in the worklist header** (no hero band, no billboard CTA row, no large empty half, no separate summary card); secondary counts/caveats/filters/sorts collapsed into the same vertical rhythm at label weight (no chip wall); **at least one real data row visible at rest**. Wired through every prose consumer: `design-handoff` step-01 §3f.4c + gate (f) clause + step-03 checklist + `state-variables.md` + brief-template §4g rule 7 and §7 in-surface block; `design-synthesize` step-04 generator rule (4); `shared/claude-design-prompt.md` item 8; `design-review-pr` step-03 **C5** with a DOM-lane sub-check; golden matrix **v5 rows 21–23**; and a WARN-only clause in `custom/githooks/check-design-brief-completeness.sh`.

**Why:** B1–B6 govern the shape of the ARTIFACT. Nothing governed the composition INSIDE the canonical render. A render can be first, dominant, correctly labelled, with the primary action unmistakably the loudest element — passing every A-rule, every §4g field, every B1–B6 rule and C1–C4 — and still open with a hero row plus a chip wall above the worklist. **B5 measures hierarchy; only B7 measures compression**, and a billboard CTA satisfies B5 *by construction*. Third silence in the same family (§8.2c which render → §8.2d artifact shape → B7 surface shape), each invisible to the layer above.

**Two findings from walking the prose-consumer table:** (1) the anti-hero rule already existed as an **AI-fingerprint bullet** in brief-template §5 — generic prose, in the `branded` branch only, bound to no surface class and checked by no review lane; that placement is why it kept slipping. (2) The hook's first marker cut matched `dashboard opener`/`chip wall` and silently **passed all five `/inbound` briefs**, which already carry an analytics-band rule banning "the generic enterprise-dashboard opener" — a real rule, but scoped to analytics bands and phrased as a ban on "three summary cards", which a hero-plus-chip-wall does not literally match. **A marker matching a PROHIBITION rather than the SPECIFICATION manufactures a false negative on the highest-risk population.** Marker tightened to the affirmative B7 phrasing; re-measured across all 44 project briefs: **6 true fires / 0 false positives** (all 6 handheld table-first briefs genuinely predate B7).

**Scope call:** B7/C5 bind by **CONTENT SHAPE, not viewport class** — any DECIDED class whose primary content is a list/table/queue/worklist, desktop included, measured at that class's own canonical viewport. Scoping it to handheld would have re-created the same silence one class over. Single-record cockpits and OPEN owner ambitions are out of scope (row 23 is the guard). **No posture changed.** Project binding: cash-recovery `docs/design-policy.md` v12 — §5 #15, §6 assertion, §8.2d binding rows. Golden rows 21–23 are **SPECIFIED, NOT YET RUN**. **The six firing briefs are NOT hand-patched** — adding a composition clause to a brief's §7 is a material revision under `brief-revision-policy` §3 and routes through a `design-handoff` re-run, not an edit.

### 2026-07-25 — fork-gaps schema v1 ADOPTED: 47 entries migrated, write-time gate ARMED (`935395f8`, pushed `myfork/custom`)

**LANDED 2026-07-25 (owner approved; single typed ledger chosen).** 47 entries migrated with zero unexplained line loss; the three validators are wired into `.githooks/pre-commit` behind an exact-match `docs/fork-gaps.md` conditional and are now NON-OPTIONAL for every edit to the register. Gate proven by injecting a blank marker and confirming the commit was refused. One field remains `unknown` (which project owns a worktree node_modules gap) — declared debt, never guessed. Two things the migration broke and the same commit repaired: the SessionStart surfacer counted `###` sub-blocks, turning 47 entries into 141 phantom "open gaps" titled Incident/Work (now level-2 only, reading closure from `state:`); and the detector's invariant test was bound to the retired shell implementation (rebound to the shared parser, 16/16, and it gained I3 — creation mode is the only failing path and it fails OPEN). The arming pass also caught a false-positive class in creation mode itself: "added line" is not "new entry", so any bulk edit re-adds every id and would have blocked every legitimate stale-open candidate; novelty is now a set difference against HEAD's pre-image.

**Original proposal note follows.** ~~**Status is deliberately PROPOSED, not shipped.**~~ The schema is the accepted working baseline pending ONE owner decision (single typed ledger vs split ledgers by scope) plus approval. The 45 live entries are untouched, the migration script is prepared but uncommitted and unrun, and the write-time hooks are designed but **not wired** — wiring them against unmigrated entries would fail every commit touching the register.

**What the schema says.** An entry becomes three blocks with three lifecycles: `### Incident` (immutable, append-only evidence — corrections and retractions included, because the contradiction is the record), `### Work` (mutable, carries a `**Status (date):**` line), and an optional `### Doctrine` block that collapses to a pointer once the reasoning is encoded in a real contract. Headings carry `id` + title only — no state blobs. A fenced-YAML header block becomes mandatory and machine-checked: `id · class · scope · target · marker · state · owner`, plus `superseded_by` / `blocked_by` / `distribution` required by state. **Markers are required except for `scope: harness`**, which is the one exemption and is enforced by pairing rather than left to convention. Six states: `open · blocked · partly · fork-fixed-distribution-owed · superseded · closed`. `blocked` is the one addition beyond the specified five (distinct triage behaviour — skip without re-deriving why; two live cases). `fork-fixed-distribution-owed` promotes to a first-class state what the register already complains about in prose.

**Why (measured, same session).** 5 of 6 entries opened were already fixed and never closed; 1 claimed RESOLVED naming two artifacts absent from disk; the stale-open detector could see 15 of 48 entries because `Marker:` was documented optional; 6 `Target file:` pointers had rotted past a directory reorg. Entry *quality* was never the problem — it is a schema that is advisory, swept by hand, and unchecked at write time. Interim: marker coverage was taken to 100% and a blank-marker false-positive in the detector was fixed, so stale-open detection works today at 28 checkable entries even before migration.

**Owed:** owner approval + the ledger-structure decision → then migration in one reviewed line-conserving commit → then the three validators wired into `.githooks/pre-commit` behind an exact-match `docs/fork-gaps.md` conditional, at which point they are non-optional for every future edit.

**Scale correction (recorded, not quietly edited).** The gap entry originally claimed "22 files already reference these standards". Measured precisely: `custom/skills/` holds 12 files of which **exactly 1** does (`bmad-correct-course/SKILL.md`, and only because the same session added the reference); the other 21 were in the **generated port** — duplicates of files already scanned. The blind spot was structural and real; the live exposure was 1 file, not 22. This closed a hole before it mattered rather than repairing damage.

**New test — `test:standards-corpus`, wired into `npm test`.** The helper's own MANDATE header briefly contained the literal `custom/skills*` + slash, whose `*/` closed the block comment and made the module unparseable; it was caught only by running it by hand. That is a bad place for a load-bearing check to live now that one helper feeds four gates, two armed — a parse failure takes all of them down on every commit. P1 asserts the module imports without a parse error; P2 asserts `{files, roots, missingRoots}` shape, the roots+missingRoots accounting invariant, and that the generated port never leaks in. **Negative-checked:** re-injecting the exact `*/` bug into a copy makes P1 throw `SyntaxError`, so the test can actually fail. Counts are deliberately NOT asserted — a corpus census would be a maintenance tax that gets deleted.

**Escalation logged (owner routing needed): SR-26 shape mismatch, cash-recovery register.** `check:scoperegister` flagged SR-26 as `route: R3-design` with a `next_artifact` pointing at a policy memo rather than a design-handoff brief. A `SHAPE-ERROR` comment is recorded in the register; **SR-26's fields were NOT modified** — that stays with its owning thread. **But the flag is contested on two grounds and must not be actioned as a settled defect:** (a) the row's artifact resolves to a `modify-design-policy` run producing `docs/design-policy.md` v10, a legitimate R3 design-lane output that **STD-SCOPEREG-001 §3 has no shape for** — the standard names only design-elevation/design-handoff → brief, so a correct policy-lane row *cannot* pass the check; (b) the row carries all three R5 activation parts (owner, observable trigger, why-not-now) **while also** naming its eventual route, which the standard treats as mutually exclusive. Both readings suggest the standard is incomplete, not the row. Owner call needed on a §3 revision adding a POLICY lane and a parked-with-known-route state.

### 2026-07-25 — STD-SCOPEREG-001: scope-register rows get a route, so registered scope can't sit inert (fork standard + correct-course producer step + a WARN-ONLY linter; **NO fan-out — rides the standing fleet sync gate**)

**The broken step (diagnosed, not assumed).** Greenfield shaping has a fully-owned chain — brief → PRD → architecture → `epics.md` → `create-story` → story file → `dev-story` → PR — where every hop has a producer, a consumer, and a machine-readable state with a defined enum. Brownfield scope change had **no producer at all**: `correct-course` is the fork's scope-change front door, but a corpus grep found `scope-register` in exactly four fork files, and inside `bmad-correct-course/SKILL.md` it appeared **once — in a parenthetical describing a use the workflow never implemented** ("correct-course used only as the scope-register front door", in the step-6 marker logic). So the register was hand-maintained; the route lived in narrative prose *outside* the table; and `disposition: accepted` + an empty linked-artifact was indistinguishable from delivered. Everything downstream (routing, actionability, audit) was unenforceable because nothing owned the row.

**What shipped.** New `custom/workflows/shared/scope-register-routing.md` (STD-SCOPEREG-001). Every row now carries `route` (closed enum `R1-capability` / `R2-bounded-local` / `R3-design` / `R4-operational-milestone` / `R5-parked`, with `R0` firing first as a *gateway* when the item changes an accepted decision), `next_artifact` (the route-appropriate SHAPED artifact — first story file / quick-spec / active design brief / milestone-block key), and `activation` (owner + **observable** trigger + why-not-now, mandatory and only on `R5-parked`). New **`correct-course` Step 4.5** is the producer; its step-6 close-out must declare each row's route and states that "recorded in the scope register" is NOT a completion. Adds the **actionability ladder** (REGISTERED / PROPOSED / DESCRIBED / SHAPED) naming the conflation directly — a proposal is actionable for an owner *decision* only, and an epic with N `backlog` story lines is ONE described item, not N actionable ones. Adds the **R4 milestone-block vocabulary** the `sprint-status.yaml` enums never had (grep: 0 hits for "milestone" in sprint-planning), with per-item `owner: operator|agent|external`, so operational proof work stops being mis-encoded as build stories.

**Enforcement (honest, and the reason this differs from its sibling).** STD-SCOPEROUTE-001 governs a prose *answer*, so it is legitimately PROBABILISTIC. This governs a **file** — so a deterministic tier exists and is taken: `tools/check-scope-register.js` (`--register` row lint · `--audit` inert-scope sweep incl. promised-but-never-produced paths · bare = fork adoption scan). It ships **WARN-ONLY**, deliberately NOT armed in `npm test` or the pre-commit fast-path — the row-shape heuristic is unproven against registers written before the standard existed, and a linter that false-blocks a commit on a legacy row is what gets gates ripped out. Route *correctness* is explicitly **ceded** (a checker cannot prove `R2` should have been `R1`) to a new nine-case golden suite `evals/scope-register-routing.md`, drawn from real rows. The `PostToolUse` re-lint on register writes is designed but ships on the hooks track — **authoring this did not deploy that hook.**

**Proof it bites.** Run against the live cash-recovery register: **24 rows, 21 formally inert** (no route at all) before backfill; 0 after, with 2 true-positive warnings retained (SR-21's active brief still says video is out of scope while the clip shipped; SR-23's Epic 10 has seven `backlog` lines and no story file). The pass also surfaced three scope items living inside other rows' prose with no row of their own, and one **overdue trigger**: SR-12 was parked pending an `/inbound` shell-ownership decision that resolved 2026-07-20 — its own instruction said "surface immediately when the decision resolves", and nothing did.

**Scope.** `custom/workflows/shared/scope-register-routing.md` (new) · `STANDARDS.md` (STD-SCOPEREG-001 registered + Recent-changes line) · `custom/skills/bmad-correct-course/SKILL.md` (Step 4.5 + close-out rule) · `custom/workflows/shared/scope-extension-routing.md` (sibling cross-link + lane→route map) · `custom/workflows/4-implementation/sprint-planning/sprint-status-template.yaml` (milestone vocabulary) · `tools/check-scope-register.js` (new) · `package.json` (`check:scoperegister`) · `evals/scope-register-routing.md` (new). Project side (cash-recovery): register routing section + 24-row routing index, sprint-status milestone/`blocked` definitions + per-item owners. Gates green (refs 0 leaks · close-out 14 adopters · completion · context-budget · markdownlint 0 err).

**Sync:** workflow + standard prose owed to the standing fleet re-sync gate — do NOT fan out from a routine session. `skills-native/_shared/` mirror regeneration rides that same pass.

### 2026-07-25 — the shared design-ingest manifest gets a multi-writer contract + a deterministic detector (fork docs/workflows + a machine-local hook; **NO fan-out — rides the standing fleet sync gate**)

**What:** new `docs/manifest-contract.md` — who may write a `design-ingest-*` / `design-implement-grid-*` ledger, when, and what a second writer must do. Four rules: (1) per-pass identity is **stamped** (`pass_id` = `<session_id>-<UTC compact>`, plus `session_id`/`started_at`), never derived by read-and-add-one; (2) records are **append-only** and `seq` is **derived** from `started_at` — a run-order table at the head of the ledger is authoritative and file order is declared meaningless; (3) a **current-editor marker** per manifest at `<main-checkout>/.claude/manifest-locks/<manifest>.lock.json` (resolved via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, self-ignoring, so it is visible from every worktree instantly and can never itself merge-conflict) — a live foreign marker forces explicit reconciliation and no session may clear another's; (4) **commit hygiene** — stage a manifest explicitly by path, never sweep it with `git add -A` / `git stash` / a sync run. Bound into `design-ingest/manifest-schema.md` (new "Multi-writer contract" section) and `design-implement/steps/step-04-apply-and-deliver.md` §5a (four rules at the point of the ledger write). Detector: `~/.claude/hooks/manifest-contract-gate.py`, PreToolUse `Edit|Write|Bash`, plus a CLI (`--acquire`/`--release`/`--status`/`--check`); 12 golden cases green, 5 of them false-positive guards; registry row added.

**Why:** the 2026-07-20 fork-gap ("SHARED, HAND-VERSIONED write-back ledger with no multi-writer contract") had already recurred once, producing `Pass 5 -> Pass 4 -> Pass 5`. **It recurred TWICE MORE during this fix** — a third time on 2026-07-25 (`667cefd`, a second `Pass 6`) and a fourth *while the migration was running*, when a live session appended `Pass 7` as the migration was renumbering a record to 7. Four collisions on one artifact; nothing lost, every time by ordering luck. The migration also produced the clean proof that a display header is not an identity: two colliding passes carried the **identical** `claude-session-20260720-171114` header while holding different `session_id`s, 11 seconds apart.

**Scope:** `docs/manifest-contract.md` (new) · `docs/hooks-registry.md` · `custom/workflows/implement/design-ingest/manifest-schema.md` · `custom/workflows/implement/design-implement/steps/step-04-apply-and-deliver.md` · fork-gaps entry marked ADDRESSED with the two new recurrences logged. Project side (cash-recovery): `design-ingest-clerk-receive.md` migrated to a coherent derived 1..8 sequence with `legacy_heading` preserved on every renumbered record, plus a CLAUDE.md section and a register claim. Candidate fix 2 (per-pass `ledger/<session_id>-<ts>.md` files) deliberately NOT taken — it removes the conflict surface but scatters the resume state and destroys the ledger as one readable narrative; the derived run-order table buys the same ordering guarantee.

**Enforcement (honest):** DETERMINISTIC that the hook FIRES on every violating tool call, and that a cross-session `--release` is refused; PROBABILISTIC that a session takes the marker, reconciles a real concurrent edit, or resolves run-order semantics between genuinely concurrent passes. **It warns, it does not deny** — promotion criteria in the contract; the marker check and the Bash matcher stay permanently warn-only.

**Sync:** workflow prose owed to the standing fleet re-sync gate (do NOT fan out from a routine session). **The hook does not sync at all** — machine-local `~/.claude/hooks/` + `settings.json`. Authoring the contract did not ship the enforcement.

### 2026-07-23 — design-implement state-render cede is now GATED, not just described (pushed `myfork/custom`, **NO fan-out — rides standing fleet sync gate**)

**What:** the "State-render coverage" cede in `design-implement` step-04 §5b.3 (added 2026-07-19, `f4062496`) was prose-only — described but not enforced. Made it mandatory: added a "State-render coverage (prod-smoke owed)" section to the §9 mandatory-section gate (equally-mandatory alongside Frame-coverage / Content-lane / Capabilities-removed / Entry-point — non-conformant if omitted when a live/local render was the done-check), plus a matching acceptance-list bullet and checklist.md item. **Why:** a live render only paints the states the seed data contains, so a passing default-state render silently leaves non-default state-variants (the mapping-queue-v8 miss: `pack_split`, open §13 drawer, claimed-elsewhere banner — zero seed rows) visually unverified; describing the cede without gating it let a report omit it and still read as "done." Same shape as the §2c content-lane cede (verification evidence can't cover a contract axis → cede-by-disclosure, don't fake). **Scope:** `custom/workflows/implement/design-implement/steps/step-04-apply-and-deliver.md` + `checklist.md`; closes the gating half of the 2026-07-16 fork-gap (now `[gating completed]`, open only on distribution). The per-state seed helper (`db:local:sample --states`) stays a non-gating project-local adjunct, unbuilt. **Self-review:** contract-dimension-gap fix at the enforcement layer, mirrors four existing equally-mandatory cedes verbatim in structure; no autonomy/provenance surface touched. **Sync:** owed to the standing fleet re-sync gate (do NOT fan out from a routine session), same as the entries below.

### 2026-07-22 — STD-HOOKACTIVATE-001 probe: resolved-path compare kills the absolute-`core.hooksPath` false alarm (`f1af3415`, pushed `myfork/custom`, **NO fan-out**)

**What.** `check-hook-activation.sh` (the SessionStart liveness probe for STD-HOOKACTIVATE-001) reported "gate is NOT firing — pushes/commits are not gated" on any repo whose `core.hooksPath` was a valid **absolute** path (git honors it verbatim; accounting-tools is configured this way). Root cause: a literal `[ "$hookspath" != ".githooks" ]` string compare that only accepted the relative form. Now resolves both the configured path and the expected `.githooks` to canonical absolute paths and compares those.

**Why it mattered (not cosmetic).** This is a DETERMINISTIC awareness probe on a *safety-critical* signal — its whole justification is that a false sense the gate is live is dangerous. The inverse (a false "your gate is OFF") is equally corrosive: it trains the operator to dismiss the one alarm that must stay trustworthy, and it nudged toward running `activate-hooks.sh` to "fix" a working gate (rewriting a valid absolute config to relative for no reason).

**Scope.** Worktree-aware: resolves against the git **common** dir's toplevel (`git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir` → `dirname`), never the worktree-local `$PWD`, so one shared absolute `core.hooksPath` reads ON across all linked worktrees — the fixed false-positive is not traded for a false-negative inside worktrees. `canon()` normalizes via `pwd -P`; older git without `--path-format` falls back to `--show-toplevel`. Husky-conflict branch (28-31) and exec-bit checks (39-44) unchanged. Per-repo configs NOT touched — the detector was the bug.

**Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom`; full fork pre-commit suite green (refs/lint/markdownlint/context-budget/close-out/completion/hook-smoke 10✓/doc-links). **NO fan-out** — the probe is a global asset run from the fork path directly (`~/.claude/settings.json:206`), not synced into the 13 projects; live next SessionStart with no re-install. `fork-gaps.md` entry carries the RESOLVED note.

**Self-review verdict.** Regression-verified across 7 scenarios (relative→silent, absolute→silent, elsewhere→warn, dropped-exec-bit→warn, live accounting-tools→silent, worktree-of-absolute→silent, husky-conflict→warn); 0 blocking.


### 2026-07-20 — depth-pass PROVENANCE disclosed: the §4d/§4e fallback can no longer manufacture its own pass (`c7a6917e`, pushed `myfork/custom`, **NO fan-out**)

Closes the `analytics-rigor-undistributed` fork-gap. **The defect was not the one it looked like.** `analytics-rigor` (§5c-2, `C-RIGOR-01`) and `decision-analysis` (§5c-3, `C-DECISION-01`) are named as the producers of brief §4d/§4e and are **authored in NO resolution root** — global / workspace / project / fork (corpus sweep). First read was "the gates are performative"; that was WRONG and is corrected here: both gates check the **rendered surface against §4d/§4e** and take those sections as ground truth, so they fire normally — they simply cannot audit the spec they measure against. The real failure is sharper: each section has a sanctioned by-hand fallback rendering an **identical-looking** block, and every consumer treats a populated §4d as evidence the pass ran. Undeclared, the fallback doesn't merely under-enforce — **it produces the evidence that enforcement succeeded**, so the only §4d the gate can fail is an HONEST one (declared gaps + a `mixed` verdict give a reviewer things to flag; a hand-waved block reads clean). Enforcement anti-correlated with the behaviour it enforces.

**Shipped (5 files; gates NOT demoted — they are real conformance checks):** `{rigor_source}` / `{decision_source}` (`skill | inline-fallback | not-applicable`) mandatory on BOTH paths in step-01b §5c-2/§5c-3 with the reason recorded on the fallback; a required declaration line in brief-template §4d/§4e; a **tier-6 DETERMINISTIC** warn in `check-design-brief-completeness.sh` for a §4d/§4e with no declaration, plus a tier-7 cross-check (a `skill` claim with no matching marker is unsupported); a **tier-7 DETERMINISTIC** `PostToolUse:Skill` marker in the `hooks.json` template that records real invocations; and the corrected "mandatory pass … enforced by C-RIGOR-01" overclaim in `analytics-archetypes.md` (wrong twice — the gate is not a rigor audit, and rigor quality is irreducibly probabilistic). `design-review-pr` step-01 now reads `rigor_source` and **escalates the human-judgment prompt to audit the SPEC** when it is `inline-fallback`.

**Honest ceiling (labelled in-artifact, not just here):** rigor QUALITY stays PROBABILISTIC forever — accurate label is **"rigor provenance disclosed", never "rigor enforced"**. `rigor_source` alone is self-reported; only the marker makes it unfakeable, and even that proves *invocation*, never quality. The marker file is absent wherever the hook isn't installed (hooks ride the onboarding track, NOT workflow sync), so the gate treats absent as **UNVERIFIABLE and stays quiet** — deliberate, to avoid noise-poisoning the gate; the hole is named in-script.

**Deliberately NOT done:** the two skills remain unauthored (separate owner decision — `tool-discovery` confirms both are genuine uncovered gaps, nothing adaptable, consumer contract already written). **NO fan-out run** — the `## Now` ⛔ fleet re-sync STOP item stands (11 blocked projects, dedicated thread only); these changes reach the 13 projects on that deliberate sync. Gates run manually: `sh -n` clean, `validate-context-budget` OK (step-01b was already over soft budget pre-edit — noted, not structurally worsened), markdownlint 0 errors / 4 files.


Newest first. **One discrete entry per wave** — `### YYYY-MM-DD — title (commit)` + a bounded paragraph (what · why · scope · delivery). NEVER a single run-on line. Keep ~12 entries here; when it grows past that, move the oldest (in newest-first order) into [`STATUS-archive.md`](./STATUS-archive.md).

### 2026-07-25 — an ingest manifest must declare what it can be TRUSTED FOR (`aa62f02d`, pushed `myfork/custom`; **NO fan-out — rides the standing ⛔ owner-gated fleet re-sync**)
**Byte-ceiling follow-up CLOSED same session (`2f1508f6`):** `step-01-ingest-design.md` split one-job-per-step into a router (`step-01`, keeps `SHARED` + success/failure) plus `step-01a-ingest-url.md` / `step-01b-ingest-bundle.md` / `step-01c-ingest-manifest.md`. A run now loads only the path it executes — URL 94→79KB, bundle →51KB, manifest →44KB — and headroom goes from 929 bytes to ~54KB. Lossless (lines 39–837 extracted by range, verified byte-identical per file); **no section renamed**, so the ~24 `URL.*` citations were not rewritten — step-01 carries a citation legend and `SHARED` stayed put to keep `workflow.md`'s `step-01 §SHARED.1a` citations valid. Gates: markdownlint 0-err/11 files, context-budget 0 blocking, validate-file-refs 0 broken/0 leaks.

**FG-2026-07-25-14 status — `fork: FIXED` · `fleet: OPEN`.** Fork: FIXED at `aa62f02d`, `manifest_grain` contract live. Fleet: OPEN — the ⛔ re-sync STOP is reaffirmed by the owner (2026-07-26), so **all 13 projects still carry the old contract and fork/fleet are INTENTIONALLY DIVERGENT** until an explicit "deploy manifest-grain contract to fleet". Consequence to hold onto: **any downstream behaviour that assumes a fleet manifest is honest at `value-exact` is UNVERIFIED** — the grain field does not exist there, so treat a fleet manifest as `summary` no matter what it claims. Register state: `fork-fixed-distribution-owed`. **Routing:** logging an FG gap is NOT authorisation to implement it (`global-bmad-workflow.md` §Autonomous-maintenance, landed `fd455e96` in this same window) — this fix shipped under an **explicit in-thread directive from the owner** to `claude-session-20260725-205653`, which named a concrete id, a target file, and the required changes, clearing that section's grounding bar. The earlier log entry was not the authorisation; the directive was.
Closes the authoring half of `FG-2026-07-25-14`. **What was broken:** the `ingest_manifest` path advertised "no download, no per-component re-catalog" and `design-implement` MANIFEST.2 was told to build its CSS catalog from the scaffold's `component×property rows` — but `manifest-schema.md` never *required* those cells to hold resolved values, and its own worked example showed `…` as an acceptable cell. So `design-ingest` could emit prose anchors, satisfy every gate (incl. the frame-completeness invariant), and leave the consumer's stated contract unmeetable; the only guard was SHARED.1 "catalog non-empty", which prose satisfies. Producer-compliant and consumer-unmeetable simultaneously, with nothing in between to object. **Why it mattered more than a wasted re-read:** a delivered, completeness-passing manifest (cash-recovery Clerk Inbound Board) restated three source facts WRONG — finding F2 asserted no `<img>` existed anywhere and the resolved thumbnail was never drawn (the source draws one in three places, and F2 then built an inference-hazard warning on that false premise), `30px` for a `26px` numeral, and a chip labelled "Can't vouch" where the source said `Gaps`. An *incomplete* manifest degrades safely (the consumer re-reads); a **lossy-but-confident** one is worse than absent, because the manifest is authoritative-by-position for exactly the fresh context that will never open the design file. All three were caught only because that run re-read the source *contrary to* the path's own shortcut. **Shipped:** `manifest-schema.md` gains a REQUIRED `ingest.manifest_grain` (`value-exact | partial | summary`; **absent ⇒ summary**, so legacy manifests degrade to a re-read and never to a wrong value), `completeness.sections_with_property_rows` + `sections_missing_property_rows`, a **Grain invariant** section (per-value consumer contract — `summary` is a LEGITIMATE manifest, since the section inventory + completeness gate are the artifact's core value; the defect is an *undeclared* summary), a **Restated source facts** section (source-reference-or-omit · no unverified exhaustive negatives · never reason downstream from a copy), and an explicit `PROPERTY-ROWS-MISSING(<reason>)` sentinel replacing the `…` the schema had been modelling as acceptable. `design-ingest` step-03 §2a classifies each cell, stamps the counters + grain, and **HALTS on `value-exact` + non-empty missing-list** (the precise lie the field exists to prevent); §2b strips restated facts pre-emit. `design-implement` step-01 MANIFEST.1a reads the grain BEFORE MANIFEST.2 and makes the source re-read a **required step** on `partial`/`summary`; MANIFEST.1b demotes the manifest's restated facts (source wins, corrections written back, exhaustive negatives are hypotheses); SHARED.1 is now explicitly necessary-not-sufficient. **Scope/delivery:** fork authoring only — the ⛔ fleet re-sync STOP stands (11 blocked projects, dedicated thread), so the 13 projects still carry the OLD contract and this is **not yet true downstream**; gap state stays `open` per the distribution-owed rule. Gates: markdownlint 0-err/3 files, `validate-context-budget` 0 blocking, fork-gap schema/targets/stale-open 0 errors. **Follow-up named, not left as a landmine:** the edit took `design-implement/steps/step-01-ingest-design.md` from 90,081 → 94,071 bytes — **929 bytes under the 95,000 hard ceiling**, so the next addition there will block the pre-commit gate; it wants a one-job-per-step split (URL vs MANIFEST is the seam), not further compression (compression was already applied once, moving the grain detail into `manifest-schema.md` behind a pointer). **Observed during the wave (not actioned):** a parallel fork session's commit `e4e2935e` swept this session's uncommitted `docs/fork-gaps.md` entry into its own commit — a live instance of `FG-2026-07-25-13`, and of the manifest-contract "commit explicitly by path" rule applying to the fork register too.

### 2026-07-18 — operator-domain-pass: §3e twin of finance-domain-pass, closes the domain-blind-cockpit gap (fork-ready, no fan-out)

**What:** new `custom/skills/operator-domain-pass/SKILL.md` (twin of `finance-domain-pass`, for OPERATOR ROLE instead of MONEY) + its wiring into `design-handoff` step-01 as **§3e**, co-firing with §3d on the `{is_processing_cockpit}` gate. §3d captured how the operator DRIVES a cockpit; §3e now captures WHO the operator is and what the surface must SHOW before it ASKS — operator role · trust boundary (may/may-not-decide + write-trust) · per-decision known-before-ask / evidence-required / forbidden-asks · top-level must-not-infer · ordering-invariants · policy-collisions. BLOCK on shape violations in frontmattered files (missing field / bad status·executor enum / malformed date·version); WARN on a newly added frontmatter-less runbook; silent otherwise. **Opt-in, fails CLOSED:** a SILENT NO-OP unless the repo declares its runbook globs in `.githooks/runbook-paths.conf` (payload-roundtrip precedent) — inert on every repo that hasn't opted in, and opting in is deliberate because layout AND frontmatter schema differ per repo. **Why opt-in, not forced:** owner decision (fork-ready + hold fan-out) — the contract is proven on one draft case (comms_dashboard `docs/cases/tfp-fba-outbound`), and inbound-flow already carries a *different* header schema (name/description) for the same TFP process; unifying that schema is a SEPARATE deliberate decision routed through this skill. **Enforcement (enforcement-expert):** deterministic tier-6 GATE at commit; awareness in the case CLAUDE.md; conservative detector (fail-closed opt-in, out-of-scope files ignored); logged override `git commit --no-verify`. **Scope/delivery:** additive fork tooling; no `config.yaml`/version bump (githooks aren't provenance-tracked workflows); pushed `myfork/custom`; **rides the already-held sync batch — NO fresh fan-out**, inert until a repo opts in. Tested 7/7 in a throwaway repo (opt-in on/off, empty marker, pass, 4 violation classes, out-of-scope ignore, `sh -n`). comms_dashboard (origin repo) converged to the marker-driven script + marker in its own repo so the eventual sync is a no-op there.

### 2026-07-11 — design-handoff producer-side spawned-surface completeness gate (`7395b91e`)
**What:** a new `design-handoff` reinforcement — NOT a product change; it hardens how the workflow enumerates its own deliverable frames. New `custom/workflows/design/shared/spawned-surface-completeness.md` (**STD-SURFACECOMPLETE-001**) defines a producer-side completeness gate, and `step-01c-topology.md` **§5h runs it immediately after §5f derives `{spawned_surfaces}`**, before step-02/03/04. The gate builds an expected required-frame set from **one basis — the same source `relational-coherence-audit` walks: Drizzle FKs + `docs/relational-coherence/relational-edges.yaml`** (§3a already reads that map to seed `{linked_records_inventory}`; the gate re-uses that read and asserts nothing was dropped on the way to the frame list). Behavior: **HARD HALT** on a required frame missing from `{spawned_surfaces}` or a displayed-but-undeclared edge (route "declare it in `relational-edges.yaml`," never invent the edge); **SOFT-WARN with a required one-line rationale** for an out-of-scope edge whose foreign record the surface deliberately doesn't display (`{surface_completeness_notes}`). **Graceful degradation** — no map / greenfield / basis genuinely absent → warn and fall back to the existing §5f + step-03 §3 self-review (`{surface_completeness_verdict}` = `unverified-basis-absent`); never a false-positive halt. New COMPLETION-checklist line for `{surface_completeness_verdict}` / `{surface_completeness_notes}`. **Why:** completeness of the §7 Surface Inventory was checked only probabilistically at the producer (§5f derive + step-03 self-attest) and deterministically only *downstream* (`design-synthesize` Gate 1f / `design-implement` §2f), which — per step-04's own assertion comment — "never bites if those consumers don't run." A run could under-read the edge map or under-derive §5f and ship a brief that silently under-enumerates frames; the missing frame is then *inferred* by `design-implement` (the thin-drawer failure the Deliverable-Completeness Principle exists to prevent). This moves the check to the cheapest place — before the brief is written. **Additive:** step-04's Block-B `frames` well-formedness assertion (§95) is preserved — §5h checks *completeness before write*, step-04 checks *well-formedness after write*; both run. **Root cause class:** contract-dimension-gap (verifiable-from-existing-evidence → add, not cede — the derivation basis is already read at §3a). **Scope:** fork-only — 1 new shared doc + 1 step file; no gate/marker/permission change; no brief produced or consumed by the gate itself (intake checks N/A); no autonomy expansion. **Delivery:** committed `7395b91e`; **NOT pushed to `myfork`, NOT synced to the 13 projects** — distribution deferred by explicit owner decision (push/sync to be decided separately). **Self-review (Mode 1):** 0 blocking — additive, single derivation basis shared with the auditor, graceful degradation prevents false-positive halts, honors the faithful-mirror + true-leaf short-circuits; all fork validators green (markdownlint 0-err, `validate:budget` OK, close-out/completion gates pass, hook smoke 10/10). Residual: step-01c is over the soft byte-budget (a pre-existing density warning §5h nudges further — the point-of-use block could later be thinned to a pointer since the full rule lives in the shared doc).

### 2026-07-10 — deterministic skip-if-dirty guard for the sync `rsync --delete` fan-out (`787b5aa5`)
**What:** `sync-bmad-workflows.sh` gains `bmad_managed_dirty()` + a pre-fan-out guard: before the destructive `rsync -a --delete` into a target, if the target has uncommitted TRACKED modifications in BMAD-managed paths (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` over `_bmad/bmm/workflows`, `_bmad/bmm/agents`, `_bmad/bmad-shared`, `.claude/commands/bmad`, `.claude/skills`), the target is SKIPPED (safe-partial) with a BLOCK line + resolve hints; `--force` overrides; `--check` previews which targets would be skipped. Golden case wired into `npm test` (`test:sync-guard`, drives the real script end-to-end). **Why:** the 13-project fan-out is the highest-blast-radius / Tier-3 action in the blast-radius autonomy ladder (`feedback-lead-on-policy-governed-maintenance`) and the only mechanically-detectable one — its "stop" had been resting on operator judgment alone; now enforced by code. **Design:** SAFE-PARTIAL over REFUSE-ALL (this workspace is perpetually multi-session, so refuse-all would never fan out); scoped to TRACKED modifications only (`--untracked-files=no`) because a blunt `git status` over-blocks the normal uncommitted-prior-sync resting state — untracked local-only content stays owned by `classify_local_only` + the manifest. **Scope:** fork-only — sync tool + `test/test-sync-skip-if-dirty.js` + `package.json` + fork-gaps close (the "distribution owed limbo" tool-safety entry → archive as RESOLVED; sibling owner-gap unblocked, still open). **Delivery:** committed + pushed `myfork/custom`; the sync tool is fork-local (run FROM the fork), so **no fan-out** is needed to make it live. Same session: the autonomy rule itself re-keyed topic→blast-radius (`f114b1d7`) with six golden-case fixtures + a load-bearing Tier-1 read-only precondition, and a global CLAUDE.md pointer. **Root cause class:** the gap was `distribution-safety`; the fix refined the original design (blunt-porcelain → tracked-only). **Self-review (Mode 1):** 0 blocking — additive guard with `--force` escape + `--check` preview, deterministic tier correctly scoped, golden case regression-locked in the gate; `bash -n` clean, eslint/prettier/markdownlint green, full `npm test` passed in pre-commit.

### 2026-07-07 — design-implement page-shell guard now covers NET-NEW pages (was drift-only) (`df3bffbf`)
**What:** step-02 §1a gains a MANDATORY net-new branch — when there is no existing page to walk, `{impl_page_shell}` is DERIVED from 2–3 sibling operational pages of the same `page_mode` (`source: "sibling-convention"`) rather than left empty; step-03 §2d diffs the design against that sibling baseline and treats the bundle's full-bleed wrapper as a **rendering artifact** (a design `full-bleed` against a capped house baseline is Tier-1 — adopt the house cap unless the `authoritative: true` `docs/design-policy.md` entry wants this surface full-width). Added a `source: "mapped-impl" | "sibling-convention"` field to the `{impl_page_shell}` shape. **Why:** the page-shell axis was a *drift* detector — its impl-side source (`{impl_page_shell}`, from §1a's walk of an existing page) is empty on a net-new page, so the §2d row silently no-op'd and the page shipped full-bleed against the capped house convention (accounting-tools `/vat-filings` sprawled edge-to-edge vs `/avask` 1400px; owner caught it post-deploy). **Root cause class:** contract-dimension-gap (missing-source-on-one-input-path — same shape as the `56d44fc9` §2f frame-inventory fix: the other input path carries the same contract in already-readable form; "no existing page is not no baseline"). **Scope:** fork-only — 2 step files + `docs/fork-gaps.md` entry (logged RESOLVED-pending-distribution). **Delivery:** fork-local commit; `myfork` push + the 13-project sync fan-out **OWED** (deferred alongside the two open design-lane gaps). **Self-review (Mode 1):** 0 blocking — house style (mirrors the §2f precedent), add-not-cede, minimal state (one field on an existing var), no brief/autonomy/grounding contract touched; markdownlint verified.

### 2026-07-06 — fork-gaps archive split: live file open-only, 309KB→26KB (gap 874) (`80c1f38f`)
**What:** `docs/fork-gaps.md` had grown to 309KB — over the Read tool's 256KB ceiling, un-readable in one pass — and its open-vs-closed state was untrustworthy (the SessionStart surfacer's count diverged from a heading grep, because closure could live in the heading OR as an in-body bold paragraph). Fixed all three of the entry's own fix directions. **(a) Archive split:** a partition script (reusing `check-fork-gaps.sh`'s exact `is_open()` so live-file and surfacer classification can never diverge; pure-partition + verbatim-preservation asserted before any write) moved the 71 resolved entries into new `docs/fork-gaps-archive.md`; the live file dropped to 26KB / 7 open entries, so it reads in one pass and the whole file IS the open backlog. **(b) Canonical closure:** How-this-works §19 rewritten — closing an entry now means tag the heading `[RESOLVED …]` **and MOVE it to the archive** (never leave a resolved entry in the live file); the surfacer switched to HEADING-ONLY classification and the old body `**RESOLVED`/`**CLOSED` scan was dropped — it had false-hidden the 874 entry itself, whose prose quotes those marker strings while describing the convention (the exact fragility the gap names). **(c)** the surfacer now lists the open headings, not just a count, so the backlog is visible without opening the file. **Why:** the register that exists to make method-friction legible had itself become illegible; every fork-maintenance session paid a grep/sed reconstruction tax (this session started with exactly that) and couldn't trust the open count. **Scope/safety:** fork-local docs + the fork-root surfacer script only — no workflow-contract change, no gate change, and hooks don't sync to the 13 projects, so **no fan-out**. Two false-open entries canonicalized in passing (design-implement unlinked-island `✅ ACTIONED`; read-only-audit `fork-complete` tracker). **Delivery:** `80c1f38f`, pushed `myfork/custom`; markdownlint 0-err, bash -n clean, surfacer verified 7 open. Self-review (Mode 1): 0 blocking — the partition is content-preserving by assertion; the heading-only surfacer is strictly more robust (an open entry can no longer be hidden by quoting a marker); no autonomy/provenance surface touched.

### 2026-07-05 — file-de-vat portal-truth gate on filed/sent + re-file block (gap 824) (`779645dc`)
**What:** the money/statutory pass the prior fork-gaps wave deliberately deferred. Two spots in `file-de-vat` trusted a tool's own OK instead of portal-truth. **step-05 §1 (declare):** after the submit action, the workflow now RE-READS portal-truth via read-only `avask_reconcile_filing`/`avask_reconcile_portal` — independent of the submit tool's return — and branches on THAT, never on `{confirmation_ref}` alone: **CONFIRMED** (row left `Provide Data` / per-doc confirmations) → emit success + mark case `sent` + mint `filed` receipt; **CONTRADICTED** (row still `Provide Data`) → BLOCKED box, no `sent`, no `filed` receipt, no auto-resubmit; **UNCONFIRMED** (reconcile unseeable) → receipt `status: unconfirmed`, case NOT `sent`, decision-line pointing at the human marker. A phase-7 guard carries that verdict and never upgrades it. **step-01 Check 6 (block):** demoted from a blind hard-block on any receipt/DB-FILED to a portal-truth reconcile — a receipt is evidence-of-attempt not proof; portal CONFIRMS → BLOCK (genuine double-file), CONTRADICTS → soft-flag+disclose+PASS (re-file a genuinely-unfiled statutory return), ambiguous/unavailable → default-stop BLOCK. **Why:** 2026-Q2 hit the failure live — `avask_file_invoices` returned OK + marked 54 invoices FILED + minted a "63/63 filed" receipt while the portal row never left `Provide Data`; that false receipt then became Check 6's authoritative "already filed" evidence and hard-blocked the re-file of an unfiled return (recovered only because the owner had portal eyes). The lie both faked success AND blocked the fix. **Enforcement (enforcement-expert):** workflow verification is correctly PROBABILISTIC (instruct the existing reconcile tool + honor the human `set-filing-status.sh` portal-truth marker, the canonical "did it file?"); the DETERMINISTIC tier is unchanged — submit pinned `ask`, PreToolUse marker gate, step-03 gate-enforced pre-file drift-catch reconcile (#1078), internal-stage dedup (#1057-62). Both spots default-stop under uncertainty, so the double-file guard is not re-admitted. **Scope/safety:** additive workflow prose, no gate/marker/permission behavior changed; no brief produced/consumed (intake checks N/A); no autonomy expansion. ### 2026-07-04 — avask-filing drift-catch: block blind re-upload of an already-staged period (`e8728d9e` + accounting-tools #1078)

**What:** a new deterministic gate + proof marker that changes the default safety posture of every future German VAT filing run. accounting-tools **PR #1078 merged**: `avask_reconcile_portal` (the read-only portal-truth reconcile, #1077) now writes a PROOF marker `.claude/filing-session/portal-reconcile-<period>.json` `{activeMatchCount, missingCount}` on every real portal scrape (best-effort; never fails the read), and the project PreToolUse gate (`scripts/hooks/avask-filing-gate.sh`) extends to the doc-uploading tools (`upload_pdf`/`fill_form`/`file_invoices`): it now **requires a fresh (30m) portal-reconcile marker** before any upload (forcing the check to run) and **DENIES when the intended set is already fully staged** (`missingCount == 0 && activeMatchCount > 0` → nothing to file → pure duplication). Conservative — a legitimate **partial fill (`missing > 0`) is allowed**, and a `source=expenses` run is exempt (reconcile is Amazon-invoice-scoped). Fork side: `file-de-vat` **step-03 §2** (`e8728d9e`, **SYNCED 14/14** — drift-catch confirmed live in accounting-tools' `_bmad`) now instructs the run to reconcile portal truth FIRST and emit the BLOCKED box before Navigate/fill. **Why:** closes the blind-re-upload gap #1075 left — a human marking a period `not_filed` would still let a blind re-upload of an already-staged set through (the 2026-Q2 / CZ6F8ETAEUI 54-doc duplication). The DB ledger (READY/FILED) is not authority; portal truth wins. **⚠️ ARM caveat:** the gate is armed only once the **MCP server is restarted with post-#1077/#1078 code** — until then `avask_reconcile_portal` can't write the marker, so the upload tools have no fresh marker to satisfy and `file-de-vat` uploads are **blocked by design (fail-closed)**. The owner override (`scripts/approve-avask-filing-override.sh`, TTL-bounded 30m, logged) exists, but the practical ability to file Q2 now depends on that restart — batch it with the #1071 MCP rebuild + smoke tests. **Enforcement classification (enforcement-expert lens):** the gate is a DETERMINISTIC PreToolUse block; the fork step-03 §2 is the PROBABILISTIC awareness tier that pairs with it. **Delivery:** #1077 + #1078 merged to accounting-tools main; fork step-03 pushed `myfork/custom`, synced 14/14 (file-de-vat is runtime project-gated to accounting-tools).

### 2026-06-30 — per-worktree `_bmad/` refresh made OPT-IN (default off) — worktree teardown/merge friction

**What:** `sync-bmad-workflows.sh --worktree` now no-ops unless `BMAD_WORKTREE_SYNC=1`. The EnterWorktree PostToolUse hook still calls it, so the default is: a new worktree inherits `main`'s `_bmad/` rather than being freshened from the fork. **Why:** in old-layout projects `_bmad/bmm/workflows/` is TRACKED, so the per-worktree freshening left ~88 dirty files in every worktree — making the harness ExitWorktree teardown demand `discard_changes` (conflating throwaway sync churn with real, possibly-unmerged work → trains the reflex that eventually discards real work). **Empirically ruled out the clever fix:** `git update-index --skip-worktree` on the freshened files makes the §A3 `git merge main` integrate step ABORT ("local changes would be overwritten") — tested in a throwaway repo. With `_bmad/` tracked, per-worktree freshness + friction-free teardown + a working integrate are mutually exclusive; the default now resolves toward merge + teardown safety. The cure for staleness is to sync MAIN (the drift banner flags it), not every worktree; per-worktree refresh stays behind the flag. **Enforcement (routed via enforcement-expert):** deterministic mechanism change (don't write the churn) over a prose "don't tear down before merge" — the per-worktree sync is a convenience, not a safety primitive. **Scope:** `sync-bmad-workflows.sh` (the `--worktree` gate) + `worktree-portability.md` §8 (the doc) + `fork-gaps.md` RESOLVED. **Distribution:** the sync script is fork-root, invoked by the global EnterWorktree hook → takes effect immediately for every project (all call the same fork script); the §8 doc ships on workflow-sync (owed). `bash -n` clean; gate verified both ways (unset → skip, `=1` → full sync); markdownlint 0-err. Self-review (Mode 1): 0 blocking — opt-in/reversible, escape hatch preserved, atomic `git commit -o`.

### 2026-06-29 — design-implement fixture-to-prod permission checkpoint (step-02b §4c) (`beee0cf2`)

### 2026-06-28 — completion-contract (STD-COMPLETION-001): finisher-disposition, Phase 1 warn-only (`8b37bc8b`)

**What.** New `shared/completion-contract.md` — the "finisher, not commentator" contract. A completion-oriented workflow's terminal step MUST declare a `completion_disposition`: `pr_merged` / `pr_open` (with the PR), `owner_gated_residue` (each blocker NAMED + why owner-gated), or `advisory` (with a why; for audit/review/triage flows or an owner-scoped analysis run). Diagnosis with no disposition is the invalid commentator exit. **Why.** The commentator failure is invisible at close-out — a well-written "here's what's wrong" reads as finished while leaving safe work undone. The disposition makes "did we finish, what remains?" a declared, checkable field. **Scope.** Third sibling of the close-out family (STD-DELIVERY-001 mechanics + STD-CLOSEOUT-001 message-shape + this disposition); references, does NOT duplicate, the upstream `finisher-drive-to-completion` / `answer-shape-and-autonomy` / `lead-dont-ask` doctrine. Registered STD-COMPLETION-001; `close-out-contract.md` §2 Status element now carries the disposition. Wired into 4 completion workflows' terminal steps: quick-dev (step-08 handoff + artifact frontmatter), design-handoff (step-04 §10), dev-story (step-10), design-implement (step-04 §9). **Enforcement (enforcement-expert-shaped).** Runtime completion is a JUDGMENT → PROBABILISTIC, not gated (a Stop-hook "did it finish?" scan = indiscriminate-detector anti-pattern); only template coverage is checkable. New `tools/check-completion-disposition.js` (npm `check:completion`) is **WARN-ONLY** — exit 0 always, NOT in `npm test`/pre-commit — soak before the Phase-2 hard gate (warn-then-gate). First soak run: 7/8 close-out adopters wired; surfaced `wire-check/step-06-handoff` as a real future candidate + 3 advisory close-outs correctly classified as expected-empty. **Delivery.** Owner-authorized Phase 1; committed `8b37bc8b`, pushed `myfork/custom`, then owner-greenlit the fan-out — **SYNCED 14/14, 0 blocked** (contract live in both layouts: standard `_bmad/bmm/workflows/shared/` + skills-native `_bmad/bmad-shared/`). **Follow-up same day (`72866c3e`, SYNCED 14/14):** closed the two coverage gaps the soak detector surfaced — wired `wire-check/step-06-handoff` (a real completion workflow: autonomously fixes loose wires + delivers its own PR) and the `design-handoff/workflow.md` orchestrator reference — so `check:completion` is now **quiet (0 likely gaps)**, the *precondition* for Phase 2. Added a `--strict` exit-1 mode to the detector (`check:completion -- --strict`) matching `validate-close-out`'s reserved-`--strict` house style. **Phase-2 gate ARMED (owner-directed, ahead of soak — `<armcommit>`):** on owner direction (three affirmatives), `check:completion -- --strict` is now wired into `npm test` + the `.githooks/pre-commit` fast-path alongside `validate:close-out` — a delivering close-out missing its disposition exits 1 and BLOCKS the commit (escape hatch: reference the contract, incl. `advisory`). Honest provenance: armed AHEAD of the warn-then-gate elapsed-soak default — 0 likely gaps at arming is the precondition, NOT a real-run soak; front-run risk = a future delivering-but-advisory close-out false-blocking until its author adds the one-line `advisory` ref; fully reversible (drop the `&&` clause from both sites). The arming commit ran the now-armed pre-commit gate green (live pass-proof). **Remaining owner-gated residue:** per-project on-disk handoff-artifact disposition check (separate hooks/onboarding track, warn-only-first). 0 blocking Mode-1 self-review; pre-existing MD034 bare-email in `producer-defect-template.md` noted out-of-wave.

### 2026-06-28 — sprint-apply v2: risk-classifier + autopilot lane (snapshot-backed, fail-closed) (`sprint-apply-gate.sh` v2)

**What:** Evolved the sprint-apply gate from a blanket human checkpoint into a deterministic RISK CLASSIFIER, to fit a hands-off operating style without surrendering the irreversibility protection. Two lanes now: OWNER-GATE (the proven v1 freeze→APPROVE-token path, unchanged) and a new AUTOPILOT lane. A proposal whose ENTIRE `files_to_change` set is classified low-risk — single repo, every path a sprint-execution artifact (`_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/` or `epics.md`; `planning-artifacts/` PRD/architecture/specs are owner-gate, not autopilot), count ≤ `AUTOPILOT_MAX_FILES` (default 6), no governance/doctrine path — auto-applies with no token; everything else stays token-gated. Three rails make "auto-apply to untracked tracker state" safe: (1) the GATE derives the class deterministically from `files_to_change` — never trusts a planner `risk_class:` self-label (advisory only, mismatch is logged); (2) FAIL-CLOSED — any parse/classify/snapshot failure, missing metadata, or unmatched path → owner_gate_required (a false owner-gate costs one token; a false autopilot is unrecoverable); (3) a deterministic PRE-EDIT SNAPSHOT to `_bmad/.sprint-apply-backups/<id>/` runs before any auto-apply, making the otherwise data-loss-class tracker files actually reversible — no snapshot ⇒ no autopilot. **Why:** the v1 gate (just proven end-to-end this same day) is correct but stops on EVERY proposal; the owner's autonomy doctrine wants safe local planning edits to flow and only governance/cross-repo/destructive proposals to halt. **Enforcement (enforcement-expert consulted):** the classifier is the safety boundary, so it must be deterministic (tier-5 gate) + fail-closed; the snapshot is a tier-7 proof precondition; the planner hint is correctly probabilistic and non-load-bearing. **Mode model:** `~/.claude/sprint-apply-autopilot.mode` = off (DEFAULT — byte-identical to v1) | classify-log (logs WOULD-AUTOPILOT/WOULD-OWNERGATE routing, NO behavior change — the soak) | on. Kill-switch `_bmad/.sprint-apply-autopilot.disable`. Hands-off end-state = gate.mode=enforce + autopilot.mode=on; ladder = enforce owner-gate → classify-log soak → autopilot on. **Scope:** global hooks track (`custom/claude-global/hooks/sprint-apply-gate.sh` → `install-global-assets.sh` → `~/.claude`); correct-course manifest gains the optional `risk_class` hint; hooks-registry updated. **Self-review/Delivery:** a 29-assertion deterministic smoke test (isolated test root, every branch) is GREEN — v1 regression preserved, classify-log changes nothing, autopilot-on snapshots+applies safe ones, and every owner-gate trigger (count>6, governance path, outside `_bmad-output`, cross-repo, snapshot-fail, kill-switch) refuses to auto-apply. Installed live on this machine; ships to other machines on next `install-global-assets.sh`. **Default-off means this wave changes no behavior until the owner flips the modes** — the autopilot switch is a deliberate post-soak flip, not enabled here.

### 2026-06-28 — generate-project-context now emits the Product Concept (grounding-discipline root fix)

**What.** `bmad-generate-project-context` (`src/bmm-skills/3-solutioning/`) now crafts a **Product Concept & End-to-End Flow** section as the FIRST section of every `project-context.md`: one-line concept, value-flow & spine, an explicit **cause/effect** (which events are pipeline OUTPUTS vs INPUTS), and a forbidden-behaviours block ("never infer the concept from the task tracker alone; never invert cause/effect"). Edits: `project-context-template.md` (new first section + `sections_completed: ['product_concept', …]`), `steps/step-01-discover.md` (new §1a — discover the concept from brief/PRD/architecture FIRST; graceful no-halt brownfield fallback), `steps/step-02-generate.md` (new §0 — emit concept first + confirm cause/effect). **Why.** Triages a real cash-recovery incident: an agent reasoned ~4h bottom-up from the sprint tracker, never loaded the brief/PRD, and inverted the product's cause/effect (told the owner the app was "waiting for eBay sales" when sales are the pipeline OUTPUT). `project-context.md` — the auto-loaded grounding file — carried only tech-stack + impl-rules, never the concept, so even loading it wouldn't have prevented the blindness. enforcement-expert design (belt-and-suspenders, both shipped this wave): the CONTENT lever (the generator emits the section by construction) AND the deterministic-DELIVERY lever — a new `bmad-project-concept-card` SessionStart hook in `hooks.json` (`src/modules/bmm/_module-installer/assets/`) that extracts the Product Concept section from each project's `project-context.md` and injects it as `additionalContext` every session (case-insensitive header match; silent when the section is absent → near-zero false-positive; rides the hook-distribution rail, upserted to all targets on sync). **Scope.** Doc/instruction-only, additive (existing sections untouched), placement-first. Forward-looking: the 13 existing `project-context.md` files gain the section only on a re-run of generate-project-context (cash-recovery hand-patched in the interim). **Delivery.** Pushed to `myfork/custom` + synced to targets.
