### 2026-06-27 — design-handoff worktree-delivery guard (fork-gap resolved)

**What.** Closed the logged fork-gap "design-handoff worktree delivery splits when artifacts are read by absolute main-checkout path." A cash-recovery `design-handoff` run from a worktree wrote all four artifacts (new brief + rationale, predecessor supersede-flips) to the MAIN checkout because the predecessor/policy were read by absolute main paths and Write/Edit continued there; the split was silent (artifacts dir gitignored-but-force-tracked) until `git add` no-opped. Three-part fix in BOTH layouts (skills-native + workflows): **step-03 §1** PREVENT — bind every read AND write (incl. the §1b flip) to `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` of the current cwd, not just the computed `{output_path}` var; **step-04 §3** DETECT — a conservative deterministic-once-run pre-stage guard that HALTs (with a copy-into-worktree + restore-main recipe) when inside a linked worktree and the brief is in the main checkout but absent here; **step-04 §3** RECONCILE — note that briefs are force-tracked-despite-ignore so a re-run's NEW files are invisible to plain `git status` (always `-f`-add by explicit path).

**Why.** "Action the fork gap." enforcement-expert consulted: deterministic GATE (the pre-stage assertion) + probabilistic AWARENESS (the §1 binding) — not escalated to a PreToolUse/pre-push hook because the failure self-announces and is recoverable, and a hook firing on every `design-*` delivery is high false-positive blast.

**Scope.** `design-handoff` step-03-generate-brief.md + step-04-deliver.md in `custom/skills-native/bmad-design-handoff/` AND `custom/workflows/design/design-handoff/steps/`; `docs/fork-gaps.md` resolution marker. Mode-1 self-review: 0 blocking (conservative guard, no autonomy/provenance/brief-consumption concerns, house-style match). `validate:budget` OK (soft warnings only, pre-existing). Pushed `myfork/custom`; synced to all targets.

### 2026-06-27 — 4 fork-gap fixes: delivery -f · edit-guard heredoc · dev-story source-of-record · sprint-status prose (`df4ad7a9`)
**What.** Closed four Open `docs/fork-gaps.md` entries — the tractable, self-contained, deterministic ones. (1) **design-handoff silent-no-op delivery**: `step-04-deliver.md` §3 and `shared/delivery-to-main.md` §3 changed `git add` → `git add -f` (the `/_bmad-output/` brief path is gitignored, so a plain add stages nothing → the push ships an empty branch that *looks* delivered) and added a post-stage assertion (`git diff --cached --name-only | grep -qF <basename> || HALT` with the `-f` hint) — silent no-op → loud self-correcting failure; `delivery-to-main` `contract_version` 1→2. (2) **Bash edit-guard heredoc false-positive**: sharpened the root cause — `bmad-bash-edit-guard` greps the entire command string including the heredoc *body*, so a `>` in the body (XML tags like `<style-examples>`, `->` arrows) matched its redirect detector and blocked a plain `git commit`. Fix in `hooks.json`: a message-bearing-VCS exemption (`git (commit|tag|notes)` or `gh <verb>` carrying a `<<` heredoc / `-F` / `--body-file` body → exit 0) inserted before the edit-equivalent grep. (3) **dev-story "source of record" mislabel**: the gap's "dead copy, delete it" premise was wrong — `src/bmm-skills/` is the plugin MARKETPLACE tree (33 skills in `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`); corrected the STATUS label and added a "two channels, do not confuse" note to the porter header naming `custom/workflows/4-implementation/dev-story/` as the porter source of record. (4) **sprint-status multi-line prose race**: new `parallel-sessions.md` §B1a — story-level prose belongs in the per-story `.md` (board stays one-line-per-key), with a blessed one-owner unit-edit carve-out for any comment block that must stay. **Why.** Owner: "address fork issues and fix." These four are deterministic and low-blast-radius; fixing them removes real recurring friction (a brief that looks delivered but isn't; a routine `git commit` blocked; an agent editing the wrong dev-story file; two sessions clobbering board prose). **Scope.** Deliberately NOT attempted: the hook-distribution keystone (a parallel session is mid-build — untracked `custom/githooks/`/`activate-hooks.sh`/`hook-activation-rail-spec.md`/`check-hook-activation.sh` + an unstaged `sync-bmad-workflows.sh`; surfacing-not-building avoided a collision), the deploy-method ladder (`deployment-to-prod.md`, high — its own session), and the project-memory worktree-slug gap (durable fix is in the Claude Code harness, not the fork). **Delivery.** Staged only my 7 files (the parallel keystone WIP left untouched); pre-commit ran the full suite (src/ touched → no fast-path) and passed incl. the hook smoke-test 9/9; committed `df4ad7a9`, pushed `myfork/custom`. **Sync to 14 owed** — `hooks.json` distributes on the installer/sync fan-out; the workflow prose (delivery-to-main, step-04, parallel-sessions) rides `sync-bmad-workflows.sh`. **Self-review (Mason-BMAD Mode 1/2).** 0 blocking: the guard change functionally tested both ways (false-positive fixed, real edits still denied); no brief/provenance touched; the dev-story fix corrected a wrong premise rather than executing a risky delete; each `[resolved]` note records the corrected understanding. Known narrow limit on the guard fix (documented): a real edit chained after the commit in the same `&&` line isn't re-scanned — acceptable for an awareness/parallel-safety guard.

### 2026-06-27 — create-agent: persona content contract — 8-section shape + human-tone floor as a soft scaffold gate (`8652d39b`)
**What.** Made the owner's 8-section persona-authoring template + "human tone" standard the canonical CONTENT shape for every new agent built by `create-agent`. New `custom/workflows/meta/create-agent/persona-content-contract.md` maps the 8 sections (role/scope · personality/tone · behavior contract · escalation/handoff · interaction patterns · style examples · knowledge/boundaries · registration) onto their homes inside the existing BMAD **XML** persona, and defines the always-baked human-tone floor (3 always-do principles: acknowledge → clarify → close-loops; 3 never-do rules: don't overrule explicit choices, don't fake certainty, don't break BMAD standards). step-03-build: the old "no placeholders, no TODOs ever" rule is reversed into a SOFT GATE — scaffold real content where inferable, write an obvious `<!-- TODO(persona): … -->` marker ONLY where genuinely un-inferable (typically §6 style examples / §4 escalation target), never invent a fact to avoid a TODO. The render skeleton now folds in the human-tone floor, an escalation rule, and a `<style-examples>` block; verification greps add `<style-examples>` + a `TODO(persona)` report; SUCCESS/FAILURE metrics rewritten (covering all 8 sections, floor always present, no invented facts). step-01-brainstorm opportunistically captures `{agent_escalation}`/`{agent_style_example}` if volunteered (never interrogated — keeps the brainstorm tight). workflow.md cites the contract as an ALWAYS rule + registers the two new vars. **Why.** Owner wanted new personas to inherit a consistent, conversational shape, but explicitly wanted create-agent to STAY autonomous and leave the agent invokable on finish — "the autonomy is a feature." The wrinkle: the fork persona is a load-bearing XML embodiment file, not an 8-section markdown doc, so the template can't be the file format. Resolving that by mapping-onto-XML + soft-scaffold-gate (vs an interactive wizard) is forced by the autonomy constraint. **Scope.** Fork only: 1 new contract doc + step-01/step-03/workflow.md in `meta/create-agent/`. No change to the XML format itself, no interactive halt, fully backward-compatible. Out of scope (named): the legacy project-only cast (Devon/Rowan/Jules) — wipe-exposed + wrapperless, a deliberate promote-into-`custom/agents/` decision, not retrofitted here. **Delivery.** Committed `8652d39b`, pushed `myfork/custom`; pre-commit fast-path green (refs/eslint/markdownlint-1047/prettier/budget/hook-smoke-9-9). **Sync to 14 owed** — the contract is prose loaded via the installed path, invisible to project sessions until sync runs. **Self-review (Mason-BMAD Mode 2, hand-authored).** 0 blocking: no brief consumption (provenance N/A); grounding gate untouched (step-01's name+lane gate intact); autonomy explicitly scoped — the gate is a scaffold, NOT an interactive halt, so the "steps 2-4 autonomous" invariant holds; worktree-safe — verified `SYNC_DIRS` includes `meta` and the sync rsyncs the whole `meta/` subtree, so the contract lands at every project's installed path (no dangling reference); context-budget — the 8-section spec is externalized to the contract file so step-03 stays lean (progressive disclosure). Follow-up: candidate to apply the same content-contract/human-tone lens to the legacy cast if/when promoted.

### 2026-06-27 — STD-ESCALATE-001: class-change escalation reflex adopted across the ecosystem (`7b8f695d`)
**What.** Authored a new shared standard, `shared/escalation-on-class-change.md` (STD-ESCALATE-001), and adopted it ecosystem-wide. The standard names one reflex: when a workflow detects mid-flow that the work has changed CLASS — scope materially larger/different than the scoped unit, a missing keystone/shared seam, a planning-not-execution delta, or a wrong-lane request — it STATES the class-change, NAMES the BMAD-default gateway (correct-course / design-router / maintenance-triage), PROPOSES the route, and PROCEEDS unless vetoed. Never a numbered menu; never silent wrong-lane continuation. §4 adds an unattended/`autonomous_mode` clause: a class-change is intent-adjacent, so with no user present to veto, the workflow HALTs and records the route rather than self-routing into a planning lane. Adoption: `dev-story` (the one execution workflow that had NO class-change reflex) gains the tripwire at step 5 (mid-implementation) and step 9 (pre-review); `quick-spec` gains a spec-time tripwire (step-01 §2e); the five workflows that already embody the behavior — `correct-course`, `design-router`, `maintenance-triage`, `design-elevation`, `quick-dev` — get one-line conformance pointers (routing logic unchanged) so the standard has real callers and a new workflow inherits the reflex. Registered in STANDARDS.md under a new "workflow behavior & routing" category. **Why.** The owner diagnosed this as systemic: good local brains (specialist workflows) but a weak shared routing reflex — workflows keep handing the coordinator role back to the owner. An `enforcement-expert` pass found the act-vs-menu response was being actively sabotaged by menu-shaped terminal steps contradicting the global act-don't-menu doctrine; the fix is to canonicalize one reflex and stop the sabotage, not add a heavier gate (hard-gating a judgment call across 14 repos is the indiscriminate-detector anti-pattern). **Scope.** PROBABILISTIC tier only — the deterministic detect→marker→Stop/pre-push backstop is registered in `docs/fork-gaps.md` as dependent on the hook-distribution rail (fork-gap #1), not hand-rolled. Out-of-scope set named explicitly: sprint-status, the planning workflows, design-implement/ingest. **Delivery.** Committed `7b8f695d`, pushed `myfork/custom`; **synced 14/14 (0 skipped, 0 blocked) at `6866108c` on 2026-06-27** — observed atomic run (`--check` showed zero LOCAL-ONLY blocks first), the escalation reflex is now live in every target by default. Pre-commit fast-path green. **Self-review (Mason-BMAD Mode 2, hand-authored).** 0 blocking: standard consumes no brief (provenance N/A); grounding satisfied (inputs are the workflow's own scoped unit); autonomy explicitly scoped decision-not-intent + the unattended clause; conservative tripwire (silent when uncertain); each conformer defers by name rather than re-deriving (policy-skill two-way-routing discipline). Follow-up: the wider-population grep sweep ran manually after the Explore agent stalled at 600s — re-verify nothing new appeared if the corpus grows; collision-hook self-flag logged for the hook owner.

### 2026-06-27 — C4: maintenance-triage design lane delegates into design-router (`2e9bc817`)
**What.** The design-routing wave's gateway review flagged that `maintenance-triage` and `design-router` were two design-lane front doors giving DIFFERENT answers for the same design want — triage re-derived a coarser route map (Claude-Design paste / design-review / design-elevation) while the gateway used the richer artifact-first set, with no reciprocal dispatch. This collapses them to one source of truth: `maintenance-triage` step-03's design lane now only **names the surface + felt want and dispatches to `design-router`**; the gateway owns the lane/altitude/depth/target+placement routing and emits the precise specialist command. Changed across the design-lane prose, the triage-spec `route:` enum (`quick-spec|quick-dev|design-router`), the suggested-next-workflow template, the hand-off display, failure modes, and the frontmatter description + Goal + architecture line; dropped the now-unused `design_review_workflow`/`design_elevation_workflow`/`claude_design_prompt` pointers, added `design_router_workflow`. `design-router` gains a reciprocal Composition note so the relationship reads both ways. **Why.** Two front doors that disagree are worse than one — the owner's thesis was a single explicit routing gateway; leaving triage's mini-router in place forked the truth. **Scope.** Fork: maintenance-triage workflow.md + step-03, design-router workflow.md. Code lane (quick-spec/quick-dev), the data-quality root-cause rule, and the straddle-split are unchanged. **Delivery.** Committed `2e9bc817`, pushed `myfork/custom`, synced to 14 (regenerates the stale `bmad-maintenance-triage` skill description from the new frontmatter). **Self-review (adversarial, fresh subagent).** 0 blocking; the one Concern — the §3 hand-off line emitted `/...:design-router {path}` (a bare artifact path) while design-router's intake grounds on surface+want and HALTs if either is missing — fixed to emit the surface+want invocation; a `system-wide` surface slot was added for policy/tone clusters.

### 2026-06-27 — explicit design-lane routing: analytics-placement-triage leaf + design-router gateway + design-handoff `--placement` contract (`8878b180`)
**What.** Closes the *implicit-routing* gap: in the design family, mode/target decisions lived inside downstream workflows (design-handoff's §5b/§5d/§5e) and only fired AFTER a target was manually picked — so the target choice partly pre-decided the answer, and a routing verdict couldn't be enforced because the specialist re-derived it. Three pieces. **(1) `analytics-placement-triage`** (`custom/workflows/design/`, 4-step) — a thin router that decides WHERE analytics on an operational page lives (`band|tab|sibling-page|remove-band|no-surface`) by single-sourcing band-belongs (§5b), topology (§5d), hierarchy (§5e) from design-handoff step-01 and shape from the `analytics-surface-architect` skill; it re-derives none of them. Emits the exact `design-handoff … --placement <verdict>` command. **(2) `design-router`** (`custom/workflows/design/`, 3-step) — the design-lane front door: a thin 4-axis classifier (lane → altitude → depth → target+placement) that walks axes in order, dispatches the analytics-placement sub-decision to the leaf, and emits a CONSUMABLE handoff (specialist + target + injected decision); classify-and-delegate, with a mode-registration table (wired/deferred) so new axes are added explicitly. **(3) The consumability contract** — `design-handoff` accepts an injected `--placement` (Input + `{injected_placement}` state var); §5b and §5d honor it and skip re-deriving the **analytics-home axis ONLY** (§5d still runs its per-item `needs-detail-route` question — the orthogonal axis the injection doesn't speak to). The `analytics-surface-architect` skill now names the leaf as a second `select` caller (closes one-way routing debt). **Why.** Owner identified that the analytics band/tab/page question was a concrete instance of a general pattern — too many design routing decisions made implicitly inside downstream workflows instead of at a small reusable gateway. Build order chosen: harden the leaf + make the decision *consumable* first, then the gateway on top, so verdicts stick instead of degrading to advisory prose. **Scope.** Fork: 2 new workflow dirs + design-handoff (workflow.md Input/state + step-01 §5b/§5d) + analytics-surface-architect SKILL.md. Additive/backward-compatible throughout (no `--placement` = unchanged design-handoff). **Delivery.** Committed `8878b180`, pushed `myfork/custom`; pre-commit green (refs/lint/md/format; context-budget warnings pre-existing). **Sync to 14 owed** — surfaced as the rollout, not auto-run. **Self-review (adversarial, step-03b ×2).** Both new flows passed a fresh-subagent adversarial pass; blocking findings fixed + re-reviewed — leaf's §5d injection scope-leak (was skipping the whole pass → fixed to analytics-home-only), gateway's grounding-gate inversion (halt-if-neither → halt-if-either), refine-map disagreement between classify/emit, and an orphaned template var. Named follow-ups: **C4** — `maintenance-triage`'s design lane should DISPATCH into `design-router` (today two front doors give divergent answers; no single routing source of truth); a §5c shape-injection short-circuit; decompose the over-budget design-handoff step-01-gather.

### 2026-06-27 — quick-dev blast-radius eligibility gate (scope ceiling) — layers A+B + warn-only backstop
**What.** quick-dev gains a real scope ceiling so a run can no longer silently outgrow "small, decided work." Three layers, labelled by enforcement class: **(A, PROBABILISTIC)** a new `custom/workflows/shared/blast-radius-eligibility.md` fragment, invoked at **step-03 §0** — the one funnel BOTH modes pass through, closing the Mode-A (tech-spec) path that previously skipped step-01's Mode-B-only escalation entirely; classifies `tiny-patch`/`contained-feature`/`not-quick-dev` on the actual intended surface and reroutes on any HARD trigger (schema/migration · auth · payments · shared-infra · over file/diff threshold), overriding `autonomous_mode` like the grounding gate. **(B, DETERMINISTIC script / probabilistic call)** `custom/scripts/quick-dev-blast-radius-check.sh` — reads the OBSERVED diff (`baseline..HEAD`), excludes `_bmad/_bmad-output/.claude/docs` churn, applies directory/filename-scoped HARD-trigger globs + per-project size thresholds; called at step-07 pre-commit. Warn-only by default (always exits 0); `quick_dev.mode: gate` exits 2 with a logged `QUICK_DEV_OVERRIDE`. Rides the existing `custom/scripts/` → `./scripts/` portable-script rail, so it auto-distributes. Config block `quick_dev:` (mode/max_files/max_diff_lines) added to `config-defaults.yaml` (sync backfills it). Plus two tightenings: step-04 self-check split into **MUST-PASS** (tests/tsc/lint/build) vs a new **MUST-OBSERVE** class (migrations · background jobs/queues · auth/permission scope · config/env) — the surfaces brownfield regressions escape through; and step-07's completion output gains an **Eligibility** echo line. **Why.** The owner flagged that quick-dev's existing escalation was Mode-B-only, linguistic (scored prompt wording pre-file-load), and evaporated under `autonomous_mode` — "not a boundary, a suggestion." A run that ships a schema/auth/payments change as "quick" is a workflow-integrity failure; enforcement-expert confirmed a non-negotiable contract needs a deterministic tier, not prose. **(C) deferred-by-design:** the agent-independent pre-push wiring is per-repo (`.githooks/`/husky vary) and does NOT ride workflow sync; kept warn-only and wired per-repo rather than bumping `onboard-project.sh` (which would mark all 13 as onboarding-drift). **Scope.** Fork: new shared fragment + new script + edits to quick-dev step-01/03/04/07 + `config-defaults.yaml` + this STATUS. **Delivery.** pushed `myfork/custom`. **Project sync owed** — the prose ships via `sync-bmad-workflows.sh` and the script via the portable-script rail; surfaced to owner as the rollout action (warn-only, safe). **Verified.** Script syntax-clean; exercised against real history — silent on a 2-file/5-line change, fires correctly on a migration commit (schema + pricing triggers), and a `listing-queue-view.tsx` false-positive on a loose `queue` substring was caught and tightened to directory/filename-scoped globs before commit. **Self-review (Mode 1).** 0 blocking — additive; grounding gate untouched and composed-with (orthogonal); conservative-detector (irrelevant-path exclusion + warn-then-gate) and logged-override anti-patterns designed in up front; honest labelling that layer A's autonomous-mode override is probabilistic and the script is the guarantee.

### 2026-06-27 — onboarding playbook v2: canonical-root identity (restamp-from-worktree fix) + version bump (`onboard-project.sh`)
**What.** `onboard-project.sh` now resolves a `CANON_ROOT` — the canonical repo root — and bases the default project name + the global memory slug on it, while in-repo writes (config.yaml stamp, ONBOARDING.md, CLAUDE.md section) still target `PROJECT_DIR`. When `PROJECT_DIR` is a git worktree (`…/.claude/worktrees/<branch>/`), `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` resolves the MAIN checkout, so identity comes from the project, not the worktree. `onboarding-playbook.version` bumped 1→2. **Why.** Found live during the cash-recovery marker demo: restamping from a worktree (the common case — the tracked marker files need a PR, which needs an isolated branch) made the script take the worktree's basename as the project name (`chore+onboarding-marker-cash-recovery`) and write the `project-onboarding-done` memory to a worktree-slug dir instead of the canonical one; I had to pass `--name` and hand-relocate the memory. The script assumed `PROJECT_DIR` IS the canonical root, but the fork's own worktree discipline pushes restamp into a worktree — same family as `bmad-artifacts-untracked-main-only`. **Scope.** `onboard-project.sh` (CANON_ROOT resolution + name default + `write_onboarding_memory` slug) + `onboarding-playbook.version`. **Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom`; pre-commit suite green. Fork-local tooling — no project sync owed. **Verified** with a REAL git worktree: restamp with NO `--name` derived the canonical project name (not the branch), wrote the memory to the canonical slug, left no worktree-slug leak, and the detector is silent at v2==v2. The `--name <repo>` interim workaround is now unnecessary. **Self-review (Mode 1).** 0 blocking — additive identity resolution with a PROJECT_DIR fallback (a brand-new onboard with no `.git` yet is canonical anyway); in-repo write targets unchanged so worktree-PR delivery still works. Resolves the `onboard-project.sh` fork-gap. **Rollout NOT done here:** the owner directed stamping the ~14 repos; the other-13 production rollout (14 PRs) is held for one scope confirmation.

### 2026-06-27 — onboarding: external-upstream-fork mode + completion marker + tutorial (`a1790903`)
**What.** Three additions to the onboarding playbook, closing the only real structural gap vs the owner's "start a new project" design. (1) **External-upstream-fork mode** — `onboard-project.sh` resolves TOPOLOGY (`standalone` | `fork-of-upstream`) by auto-detecting an `upstream` remote or an explicit `--upstream <url>`, records it in the marker, and adds a CLAUDE.md "Upstream fork — sync safety" section; a new global PreToolUse/Bash guard `bmad-upstream-guard.sh` self-gates on the topology stamp and denies `git push upstream` / force-push / `reset --hard upstream/*` (logged override `BMAD_ALLOW_UPSTREAM_PUSH=1`). (2) **Completion marker** — an `onboarding:` stamp in `_bmad/bmm/config.yaml` (`playbook_version`/`onboarded_at`/`topology`/`guarantees`; single source `onboarding-playbook.version`), a generated `ONBOARDING.md`, and a `project-onboarding-done` memory; `--restamp` rewrites the marker only (no clone/sync). (3) **Teach-by-doing** — new `bmad-onboard-tutorial` skill (safe sync dry-run + first feature branch/tests/commit); `bmad-onboard` updated for fork mode / marker / `--restamp` / tutorial hand-off. **Why.** A cold session could not tell a fully-onboarded repo from a pre-playbook one, nor detect a playbook bump; and a personal fork had no guard against clobbering its external upstream. **Enforcement (enforcement-expert lane).** The marker's detection tier is `check-onboarding-version.sh`, a SessionStart sibling of `check-*-drift.sh`: DETERMINISTIC delivery of awareness, but CONSERVATIVE — **silent on a missing stamp** (the ~14 pre-marker repos would otherwise false-fire every session) and speaks only on a present-but-stale stamp; the machine-checkable stamp in config.yaml is the source of truth, ONBOARDING.md + memory are human-facing projections generated from it. The upstream guard is a deterministic GATE with a logged override; `install-global-assets.sh` registers both hooks idempotently. **Scope.** Fork root (`onboard-project.sh`, `onboarding-playbook.version`, `check-onboarding-version.sh`), `custom/claude-global/` (the guard hook + both skills), `install-global-assets.sh`. **Delivery.** `a1790903` pushed `myfork/custom`; pre-commit suite green; global assets activated locally (2 hooks registered + verified, both skills installed). Fork-local tooling — **NO project sync owed** (lives in the fork / `~/.claude`, not distributed to the 14). **Self-review (Mode 1).** 0 blocking — additive; no brief/provenance; worktree-safe (hooks distribute on the install/`~/.claude` track, explicitly not via the project sync); the conservative-detector and logged-override anti-patterns were designed out up front. A `set -euo pipefail` abort (helper functions ending on a false `[[ ]] &&` one-liner) was caught by the self-test and hardened before commit. 19/19 isolated checks pass. **Held:** the fan-out is N/A here (fork-local), but the owner's broader onboarding wave still defers the 14-target *content* sync of other in-flight changes.

### 2026-06-27 — sync deadlock fixed: content-hashed local-only manifest (`4a5f590b` + `92cb6762`)
**What:** the pre-sync safety gate blocked on any file present in a target but absent from source — unable to tell a propagated deletion from genuine local work — so removing one `shared/` standard deadlocked all targets (`Done: 1 synced, N blocked`). The sync now writes a per-project manifest (`_bmad/_config/sync-manifest.txt`, `sha256 TAB relpath`) of what it delivered; a target-only file is PURGEABLE only if it is in the manifest AND its bytes still match the recorded hash, and every other state (locally edited, absent entry, invalid hash, no manifest, whole source dir missing) → BLOCKING. **Why:** removal/rename of a standard is a first-class canon op that must propagate, but never at the cost of deleting local work. **Scope:** `sync-bmad-workflows.sh` only (hash helpers + `compute_source_manifest` + `classify_local_only` + manifest write + manifest-aware `--check`/sync gates + a blocked message that names the file(s) and the `--pull`/`--force` remediation; `shasum` added to the dep check). Additive; no brief/provenance; worktree-safe (distributed by sync). **Delivery:** base fix `4a5f590b`, then hash hardening `92cb6762`, pushed to `myfork/custom` via an isolated-worktree cherry-pick to avoid a shared-index race with a parallel session. **Verified:** `bash -n` clean; 11/11 isolated classifier tests incl. the edited-and-removed case (now BLOCKING) and an invalid-hash case (fail-closed); `--check` clean across all 15 targets. **Self-review (Mode 1):** 0 blocking — deterministic gate, fails closed in every uncertain state; the one fail-open (name-only manifest) was surfaced by an `enforcement-expert` pass and closed with the content hash *before* rollout. **Rollout DONE (2026-06-27):** pilot inbound-flow validated → full sync of the remaining 12 (`Done: 15 synced, 0 blocked`); every old-layout manifest verified 195/195, 0 malformed; cash-recovery skills-layout = gate N/A; manifest gitignored per project. Resolves the top open fork-gap.

### 2026-06-26 — prod-readiness-charter: the lifecycle layer above deployment-to-prod (`53a8d810`)

**What.** New shared `custom/workflows/shared/prod-readiness-charter.md` — the policy layer ABOVE `deployment-to-prod` / `delivery-to-main`. Those own a project once it's set up; this owns the three lifecycle moments around being set up: (1) greenfield CROSSING to prod with no deploy contract, (2) a live brownfield that NEVER had one, (3) a contract DRIFTED from the fork canonical. For each: a conservative detection signal, the satisfying artifact, and the action — plus a three-tier enforcement design (SessionStart awareness · PreToolUse deploy gate · CLAUDE.md inline reactive guardrail) on the insight that prose doesn't enforce, hooks do. Memory discipline declared as the parallel domain (same three states vs the global `memory-library-discipline`). **Why.** A live inbound-flow incident: the project CLAUDE.md carried a stale manual-`railway up` instruction (run from `inventory-manager/` when the service Root Directory makes that fail) — textbook State-3 drift that cost ~8 failed deploys to rediscover. The deploy CONTRACT was standardized; nothing detected that a project's deploy DOC had drifted, or that a live project lacked one entirely. **Scope/delivery.** Phase 0 only — policy doc, zero blast radius. The detection probes, a `maintenance-triage` setup lane, and the enforcement hooks are designed-not-built (§Rollout); each is independently revertible and gated on the prior. §3 conservative-detection explicitly excludes deliberate `bmad_contract: skip` and pre-launch greenfield from "gap" — a false flag across 13 projects erodes hook trust. Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking (no brief consumption → provenance N/A; not input-accepting → grounding gate N/A; worktree-safe — flags that hooks distribute on a track SEPARATE from `sync-bmad-workflows.sh`; context-budget — single doc, 3-state matrix tabled, points to siblings). Committed `53a8d810`; pre-commit suite green. **Pushed `myfork/custom`** (`ff3ca9a4`); **sync to 13 DEFERRED** until the Phase-1 probe exists (a read-only mirror nothing reads is churn for no value). Follow-ups: stamp `contract_version` on `deployment-to-prod.md` (State-3 MVP signal); author the Phase-1 SessionStart probe; the Phase-2 `maintenance-triage` lane; build the memory parallel only after deploy detection proves accurate.

### 2026-06-22 — diagnostics gate: "prove, don't assert" on the implementation verification gate (new `shared/diagnostics-gate.md` + 3 wired callers)

**What.** New canonical shared standard `custom/workflows/shared/diagnostics-gate.md`: when ANY new diagnostic (type error, "cannot find module", lint/compile failure) surfaces after an edit, merge, or worktree teardown, the verification gate is **RED until a re-run IN THE CURRENT CHECKOUT proves zero errors** — binary resolution (prove-green or treat-as-failure), no "probably stale" middle state. Wired into all three implementation deliver-gates: dev-story (step-8 validation gates), quick-dev (step-04-self-check §2), design-implement (step-04 test plan). **Why.** Owner-caught live incident (cash-recovery): after a worktree teardown, 5 "cannot find module" diagnostics were declared "stale" by *reasoning* (referenced the removed worktree path, build had passed earlier) and the run reported "gate green." They were in fact stale — but asserting it instead of proving it is exactly how a genuinely red gate ships as green. The reasoning is a hypothesis; only a re-run is a result. **Scope/delivery.** Adjacent to the 2026-06-22 acceptance-lane entry below — same "build green ≠ verified" failure family, but at the *diagnostics* layer rather than the *AC* layer; that entry already flagged the dev-story done-gate's too-weak signal, this hardens the diagnostics half. Root-cause class: `silent-partial-implementation` (a verification claim made without per-result accountability). Generic dev-quality rule → promoted to the fork so all 13 projects inherit it on sync. The cash-recovery project `CLAUDE.md` carries the project-specific deploy-answer-shape sibling fix separately. **Push `myfork/custom` + `sync` (13 targets) owed.**

### 2026-06-22 — code-review acceptance lane: verify each AC against CURRENT code, not the diff (XML overlay + skills-layout tracked patch)

**What.** The code-review Acceptance Auditor must verify EACH acceptance criterion against the current codebase state, not the diff. **Why.** Diff-scoped acceptance review is structurally blind to an AC that was never implemented (no change → no diff). Live incident: Story 1-4 (cash-recovery) was marked `done` with AC4 (persist reimbursements by reimbursement-id) + AC1 (persist Customer-Return `status`) never delivered — no reimbursements table, no `units.status` column — and the Epic 1 batch review missed it; green build + ticked tasks passed because no test asserted the artifacts exist. **Scope/delivery.** The fork's XML overlay (`custom/workflows/4-implementation/code-review/instructions.xml`) ALREADY had the correct current-state AC check ("search implementation files → IMPLEMENTED/PARTIAL/MISSING → MISSING=HIGH"); the v6.8 **skills-layout port silently dropped it** (→ the pilot ran the diff-scoped version). So this is a carry-over, not a new invention. Strengthened the XML overlay with a test-artifact discipline (point 5: prefer a TEST per load-bearing AC; flag untested ACs). Applied the full fix to the cash-recovery pilot's `.claude/skills/bmad-code-review/steps/step-02-review.md` (auditor gets project read access + verifies each AC against current code + IMPLEMENTED/PARTIAL/MISSING + test-artifact). Recorded as a Phase-4 tracked patch in `MIGRATION-v6.8-skills-plan.md` so the eventual port preserves it. Recurrence note: the **dev-story "done" gate** has the same too-weak signal (tasks ticked + build green ≠ ACs verified) — flagged for the dev-story port. Root-cause class: `contract-dimension-gap` (missing current-state axis) on the skills-layout copy = `upstream-fork-mismatch`. Same pattern as the earlier subagent-conflation fix (`bfac9d1e`). **Push `myfork/custom` owed; `sync --only cash-recovery` already carries the in-project pilot edit (made directly).**

### 2026-06-22 — dev-story parallel-safe story CLAIM + RECONCILE (§C in `shared/parallel-sessions.md` + dev-story SKILL wiring)

**What.** A parallel-aware claim + reconcile protocol for sprint-driven implementers (dev-story). New **§C** in `custom/workflows/shared/parallel-sessions.md`: (C0) source-of-truth rule — the story-file `Status:` line is authoritative for the story lifecycle, `sprint-status.yaml` is the shared **claim ledger**, evidence wins on disagreement; (C1) the **claim token** — an inline YAML comment on the existing `in-progress` value (`# claim: owner=<user> session=<branch-slug|pid> at=<iso8601> baseline=<sha>`), so `development_status[key]` stays a clean `in-progress` for existing readers; (C2) **atomic CLAIM** — re-read fresh, flip+token, re-read to detect a lost race, and **REFUSE** a story held by a live session (skip to next free story); (C3) **RECONCILE** on entry — heal both ledgers to evidence for the "done-but-unchecked" class, reset the "zombie" class to `ready-for-dev`; (C4) **dead-claim detection** — worktree-branch-gone + pid-dead + baseline==HEAD + no-commits = zombie, ambiguous → skip (never steal). Wired into the source-of-record `src/bmm-skills/4-implementation/bmad-dev-story/SKILL.md`: a top-level CRITICAL, Step 1 claim+reconcile (both discovery paths), Step 4 confirm-don't-reclaim (+ writes/confirms the token, HALTs on a lost race), Step 9 releases the token on `review`. Sprint-status template documents the convention. Validator fix: `bmad-shared/` → `validate-file-refs.js` `INSTALL_ONLY_PATHS` (the porter's `_bmad/bmad-shared/` home has no `src/` twin, so the §C ref isn't a broken ref). **Why.** Two live cash-recovery incidents — Story 2.8 zombie claim (claimed, baseline set, zero commits, holder vanished — indistinguishable from real WIP) and Story 4.5 done-but-unchecked drift (sprint `done`, story `review`, empty boxes, code merged). Greenfield dev-story had no atomic claim and two un-reconciled state records. Root class `contract-dimension-gap` (the parallel-coordination axis — who holds a story, and reconciling the two ledgers — was unmodeled for dev-story; the `d5b2edc9` wave scoped it out as base/skills-layer). **Scope/delivery.** Composes with §A1's worktree (the claim `session=` REUSES the worktree branch slug — one identity, no double-warn). Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking (conservative refuse-over-steal; no brief/provenance; worktree-safe — §C ships via `_bmad/bmad-shared/`). `validate:refs` 0 broken · `lint:md` 0 · `validate:budget` clean on both touched files · porter regenerates `_shared/parallel-sessions.md` with §C. **Distribution honesty:** the shared §C reaches ALL projects on next sync; the dev-story SKILL wiring needed to reach skills-layout projects, which `deliver_skills_layout_project` ports only from `custom/workflows/`. **Closed in the same wave:** dev-story PROMOTED to `custom/workflows/4-implementation/dev-story/` (`workflow.md` `name: dev-story` + `customize.toml` + `checklist.md`), mirroring quick-dev — port engine now emits `bmad-dev-story` (30 ports) and the sync rsyncs it to `.claude/skills/bmad-dev-story/`. `src/bmm-skills/` copy retained as dual-layout source of record. Piloted + verified on cash-recovery (SKILL.md carries claim/reconcile; `_bmad/bmad-shared/parallel-sessions.md` carries §C). Pushed `myfork/custom`; full 15-sync deferred (pilot only).

### 2026-06-21 — deploy.autonomous opt-in: agent-owned deploy (`41076d99`)

**What.** Added a `deploy.autonomous` config dimension so a project can opt the agent into OWNING deploy choices/questions end-to-end (fresh `origin/main` checkout, verify deploy target, apply ADDITIVE migrations) instead of the default owner-only "state-and-stop" posture — still gating DESTRUCTIVE migrations. `deployment-to-prod.md` gains the "agent-owned deploy" override clause; `onboard-project.sh` now generates `deploy.autonomous: true` so new projects auto-configure it.

**Why.** Root class **upstream-fork-mismatch**: a cash-recovery owner had to *teach* a session to deploy autonomously, then asked why onboarding didn't auto-configure it. The fork sets `autonomous_mode: true` but that flag (a) only governs workflow menus and (b) had NO deploy analogue — deploy autonomy was tribal knowledge / a per-project memory. This makes it a first-class, onboarded config knob. Non-breaking: absent the flag, owner-only posture is unchanged.

**Scope/delivery.** Fork: `custom/workflows/shared/deployment-to-prod.md` + `onboard-project.sh`. Committed `41076d99`, pushed `myfork/custom`. **Owed:** `sync bmad` to propagate the updated contract to the 15 targets' `_bmad/bmad-shared/` (deferred — 6 sessions active; recommend running when quiet). cash-recovery's own `_bmad/bmm/config.yaml` flag set separately (project-owned, not synced). Self-review (Mode 1): autonomy posture gates the one destructive case; opt-in default preserves existing safety; pre-commit suite green (test:refs/install/urls/channels/lint/budget).

**Follow-up (code-review autonomous gate — sibling to this complaint, NOT yet done globally).** Same root class: the 44 BASE BMAD skills delivered by the v6.8 install (incl. `bmad-code-review`) carry ZERO `autonomous_mode` gating, so on a fresh skills-pilot project they HALT interactively and the dev must hand-patch — exactly the create-epics case (`e8d48b4c`). cash-recovery's `code-review` was hand-gated this session and IS durable there (it's a base-install skill, not a fork port, so the skills-native sync never overwrites it). The global fix = promote `code-review` into `custom/workflows/4-implementation/code-review/` (workflow.md+steps), convert its skills-shape path refs to the porter convention (`{project-root}/_bmad/bmm/workflows/.../steps/` so the porter flattens correctly — the round-trip hazard), gate the HALTs, run the porter, diff-verify against the known-good cash-recovery gate, then sync. Deferred from this wave as a careful standalone pass (15-project blast radius; the convention conversion must be diff-verified before shipping). Broader: a one-time sweep of all 44 base skills for interactive HALTs that should gate.

All 72 wrappers structurally well-formed; the command loader registered all 72 `/bmad:bmm:workflows:*` with correct descriptions. **Push `myfork/custom` owed.** No other-project sync owed (the 13 old-layout projects already have native colon commands). Follow-ups: (orphan-cleanup) add stale-alias removal to the skills-layout path; (agent-namespace) `bmad-agent-*` skills alias under `workflows/` not `agents/` — harmless, noted; (fork-wide) migrating projects inherit aliases automatically.

### 2026-06-20 — onboarding root-fix: single-track delivery default + skip-mode deploy posture + git-merge guard (`6b078d84`)

Owner friction, traced to the root: every implement/dev-story run ended by handing the owner two recurring **standing-policy** decisions — "want me to deploy?" and "local main is N ahead, reconcile?" — and the cash-recovery onboarding had baked both in. Two template/onboard defects mint them in every onboarded project. (1) The CLAUDE.md template's `ALWAYS Deliver Your Work` section shipped BOTH a PR option AND a `git merge`-into-local-main **"Option B (solo projects)"** plus an unresolved `<!-- TODO: Choose ONE delivery method -->` — so a solo project reads local-merge as blessed, which is the canonical **origin/main divergence** (domain Stories pile up on an unpushed local main while UI ships via PR; cash-recovery hit 14-ahead this very session). (2) `onboard-project.sh` hardcodes `deploy.bmad_contract: skip`, but the template's Deployment section carried **no posture for the skip case** — so each session improvised a "deploy?" question. Root class `contract-dimension-gap` (the delivery-method and deploy-posture dimensions were unspecified at the template layer; the friction was a *missing standing decision*, not a per-run choice). Fix at the root: template `bmad:deliver-work` deletes Option B + the TODO and mandates a single PR-to-origin track with an explicit "never `git merge` into a local main you don't push; local main only fast-forwards from `origin/main`" invariant; template `bmad:deploy-contract` gains a skip-mode posture clause ("after merge, state deploy status and STOP — never ask"); `onboard-project.sh`'s deploy comment points at it. **Enforcement, not just prose:** new `bmad-single-track-guard` PreToolUse Bash hook in `hooks.json` blocks `git merge <branch>` while on `main` in a BMAD project, ALLOWING `git merge --ff-only origin/main` / `--abort` / `--continue` — unit-tested across 8 command shapes, JSON-validated, survives `format:fix`. **Two-layer distribution reality (verified by reading `sync-claudemd-sections.py`):** the hook is `jq`-upserted into all 15 projects' `settings.local.json` on next sync (enforcement everywhere), but the CLAUDE.md prose edits are **add-only** — `section_present` skips any section whose marker/heading already exists, by design, to preserve per-project customization — so the prose reaches NEW projects via the template but NOT the existing 15's already-present sections. That's acceptable: the hook is what prevents the divergence; prose is secondary. Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking (edits additive; worktree-safe — distributed via sync; no provenance/autonomy concerns; the hook is halt-by-default with an `--ff-only`/`--abort`/`--continue` escape). Delivery: committed `6b078d84`, pre-commit suite green (the single-track guard intact + valid after lint-staged); **push `myfork/custom` + sync owed.** Follow-ups: **(1) RESOLVED (`44898203`)** — instead of a 15-repo CLAUDE.md PR storm, the skip-mode deploy posture (state-and-stop, never ask) + the single-track delivery rule were added to the **shared `deployment-to-prod.md` contract** (`custom/workflows/shared/` + skills-native `_shared/`), the doc every project's CLAUDE.md already defers to and which syncs to all 15 — so the posture reaches existing projects without touching their heterogeneous CLAUDE.md prose. **(2) RESOLVED — no promotion needed:** `dev-story` does NOT local-merge (or deliver at all) — it implements, updates the story file, sets sprint status to `review`, and stops; delivery is deferred to CLAUDE.md convention, now single-track + hook-enforced. The earlier "dev-story local-merges" assumption was wrong. (3) 13 pre-existing markdownlint errors in `custom/claude-global/` skill-backup files (bare URLs + emphasis spacing), unrelated to this wave — still open.

### 2026-06-20 — parallel-sessions: worktree + parallel-reconcile protocol for implement/review workflows (`d5b2edc9`)

The fork's implement/review workflows assumed one linear session. In real multi-agent projects — observed live on cash-recovery, where one session delivered Epic-2 stories 2.1–2.4 while a second drove Epics 1+3 — **every** delivery hit a barrel / `schema.ts` / migration-number conflict and `sprint-status.yaml` was edited by both sessions at once, and none of the resolution lived in the workflow text: quick-dev never called `EnterWorktree` (it only DETECTED a worktree at deliver time), `step-07` HALTED on a merge conflict ("report the error and halt"), and code-review's working-tree `git diff` is EMPTY for an already-merged story. New `shared/parallel-sessions.md` is the missing layer. **§A src-editing:** enter a worktree from LOCAL `main` (origin lags because parallels merge locally), integrate the advancing `main` before delivery, **RESOLVE-don't-halt** the mechanical collision classes (barrel re-exports → keep both · additive `schema.ts` tables → keep both · two `0001` migrations → take theirs + regenerate yours as `0002` · `sprint-status` per-key edits), re-gate, and halt ONLY on a genuine *semantic* conflict; local-merge vs PR by `git remote`. **§B artifact:** per-key `sprint-status` edits + explicit lane claiming; no worktree (`_bmad-output` is hook-allowlisted — a worktree would wrongly isolate it). Composes with `worktree-portability` (path mechanics) + `delivery-to-main` (artifact/PR) — cross-referenced, not duplicated. Wiring is thin point-of-use pointers so the workflows stay budget-clean: quick-dev step-03 enter-worktree-before-editing hook + step-07 integrate-first / delivery-mode pick / halt→resolve-per-§A4; code-review recovers a delivered story's diff via its `baseline_commit` + `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` path resolution. Scope = **custom lane only** (quick-dev, quick-spec by-reference, code-review); dev-story + create-story/create-epics are base/skills-layer and were deliberately left for a follow-up (owner chose custom-lane-only). Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking; 2 Concerns (the migration-resolution command is drizzle-specific — the *class* generalizes; quick-spec is artifact-only and intentionally unwired). Delivery: committed `d5b2edc9`, `validate:budget` green, pre-commit suite green; **push `myfork/custom` + sync 15/15 owed** ("then we re-sync" — owner-triggered).

The owner asked to check git history first; `git log -S` showed it was a deliberate guard from a prior PR (keep delivered multi-parcel orders on a separate path). That forced a deeper read which proved the removal safe only because a downstream guard still covered the original case — and the owner asked to institutionalize the "moment of reflection." The failure being closed: deleting load-bearing guard code that reads as redundant, silently re-opening the bug it fixed.

**Scope.** 3 files in `custom/workflows/implement/quick-dev/`: workflow.md (1 CRITICAL RULE), step-03-execute.md (+40 lines, new PRE-FLIGHT), step-04-self-check.md (§6 checkbox + FAILURE MODE). ### 2026-06-18 — design-ingest: cope with a superseded handoff — tolerant supersede-awareness (`354be3f9`)

**What.** `design-ingest` and `design-implement` gained supersede-awareness; previously neither had any (they're downstream of the brief consumers, so brief-revision-policy §5's refuse contract never applied to them). `design-ingest` step-01 now derives `{target_slug}` and resolves `{handoff_supersede_status}` (active | superseded | no_brief | ambiguous) by matching the slug against briefs in `implementation_artifacts`, then stamps `ingest.supersede_status`/`superseded_by`/`source_brief` into the manifest; step-03 leads the pause with the heads-up. `design-implement` reads the stamp at intake.

**Why.** Owner: "give design-implement a handoff that may have already been done, but it's been superseded… it does ingest, cope with supersede." The failure being closed: handing the old handoff in either hit a bare "already applied (N/N)" no-op that hid the existence of a newer version, or — worse — applied stale deltas that regress the surface back to the superseded design, silently.

**Scope.** 7 files: design-ingest workflow.md (state vars + Critical Rule) + step-01 (resolve) + step-03 (stamp + pause) + manifest-schema (receipt fields + Supersede-stamp section); design-implement workflow.md (Input Resolution branch + state vars) + checklist; brief-revision-policy.md (§6 amend + new §8). Design call: **tolerate, don't refuse** — ingest is non-destructive so it stamps + reports; the only hard gate is `design-implement`'s apply-time confirmation on a superseded-with-deltas run (applying = INTENT autonomy). diff-the-two parked as a future workflow; no_brief honest-limit on raw-URL runs documented.

**Follow-on (`d848e916`).** Extended `design-implement` to cope on **direct URL/bundle runs** too (the original wave only handled the stamped ingest_manifest path). step-01 §SHARED.1a resolves supersede independently on those paths — slug from the frame inventory, matched against briefs, same contract as ingest step-01 §5 — and on `superseded` SURFACES + HALTS before the apply pipeline (a direct run has no prior dispositions, so it's effectively the with-deltas case). All three input paths now cope. Policy §8 design-implement bullet broadened to "copes on every input path."

**Delivery.** markdownlint 0; fork test suite green (pre-push); pushed `myfork/custom`; synced 14/14 (re-sync owed for `d848e916`).

**Self-review (Mode 1 on own draft):** 0 blocking, 0 concerns. Autonomy scoping is the load-bearing call and it's correct (apply-superseded = intent → HALT even in autonomous mode); backward-compat default (absent stamp ⇒ no_brief) matches the policy's absent-field posture.

### 2026-06-17 — design-implement: drilled-frame COMPOSITION axis (§2d-bis) — the drawer analog of page-shell (`d0c4e368`)

**What.** New §2d-bis Frame-composition grid row for every drilled frame (detail/create/§13-lookup), plus step-02b recomposition surfacing, new state var `{frame_composition_deltas}`, Critical Rule, step-04 success-metric + failure-mode, checklist item. **Why.** The owner showed two live screenshots (Claude Design supply-order drawer vs the built drawer) and asked "how does it not notice the visual deltas." Root cause `contract-dimension-gap`: the component sweep compares each section's inner pixels and §2f-bis certifies each section *exists*, but NEITHER compares how a drilled frame is **composed** — section order, grouping under named headings, and the frame's own header/footer chrome. So a drawer can ship with every inner component "matching" while it's wholly recomposed: design `Cost & sourcing` / standalone `Lifecycle` / `Related records` → impl `Economics` / status folded into the header / combined `Routing & source`, plus a black-vs-blue footer button. This is the page-shell blind spot (PR #2017) one level deeper, inside the drawer — and the frame's footer is the sharpest edge (not a cataloged section → no grid row at all). Compounded by a reporting flaw: the run halts at step-02b (capability preflight, which by construction never looks at pixels) and squashed the whole recompose into one line "#8 drawer · DEEPENED/treatment · buildable: yes" — so the owner, reading that phase, correctly saw nothing acknowledging the visual magnitude. **Scope.** step-02b §3 flags every materially-recomposed present frame into `{frame_composition_deltas}` and names it in the preflight (so a scope halt shows the magnitude, not a throwaway treatment line); step-03 §2d-bis emits a Frame-composition row per drilled frame comparing section order + group naming + header/footer chrome, Tier-1 on divergence, counted in `{delta_count}`, the footer explicitly in scope; workflow.md Critical Rule + state var; step-04 metric + failure mode; checklist item. Add-not-cede — the design composition is in the manifest section inventory (render order + group titles + chrome) and the impl composition is in the frame map step-02 already builds. **Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom`; budget linter green (hard gate OK). Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking — no brief consumption (provenance N/A); add-not-cede (verifiable from evidence in hand, like page-shell); autonomy scoped (it flags + builds the handoff's composition, never infers intent); section-existence stays §2f-bis (no double-count). **Sync owed (13/13).**

### 2026-06-17 — design-implement: resumable + checkpointed apply for large ingest manifests (`0fefe11c`)

**What.** design-implement's APPLY half (step-04) made resumable and self-checkpointing on an `ingest_manifest` run, with an optional `{frame_scope}` slice override. New state vars `{frame_scope}` / `{resume_prior_dispositions}` / `{run_completion_mode}`; new Critical Rule "Resumable apply on an ingest manifest"; step-04 §5a (persist-as-you-go + frame-boundary checkpoint), RULES note, §9 checkpoint/resume report branch, success metric + 2 failure modes; step-03 §2f-bis resume/scope budget note (skip delta-compute for already-applied/out-of-scope rows); step-01 manifest gating resume-read; checklist Application item. **Why.** The owner asked whether the apply of a large reviewed manifest (the real case: a 13-frame / 48-section supply-orders blocked-stock manifest) is smart to hand off "in one shot," and asked to *add* the ability for the agent to **close out when context is too tight and recommend a fresh session**. The prior wave (`293dfcc9`) fixed the INGEST half's context overflow via per-frame fan-out + a durable manifest; this is the symmetric fix for the APPLY half — a single-window walk over 48 sections hits the compaction trap (`context-budget-overflow`) and silently marks rows applied that were never verified. **Scope.** The manifest IS the durable progress ledger (its grid scaffold carries per-row status), so the fix is: apply frame-by-frame, persist each frame's dispositions back into the manifest at its boundary (before any auto-summarization), auto-resume by skipping `✓ applied` rows so re-invoking the *same command* in a fresh session continues, and self-checkpoint at a frame boundary (soft budget ~one heavy frame or ~10–12 sections) — a clean terminal exit that still delivers the slice (commit→PR→merge + force-add the updated manifest) and prints the resume command, NOT a wait-for-input halt. "One-shot" ergonomics preserved (re-run the same command); the agent slices itself. URL/bundle runs unaffected (single-pass as before). This is the context-budget decision-rule's "long sequential build → per-phase fresh context + durable manifest" remedy applied in-tool. **Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom`; budget linter green (hard gate OK; step-04 carries a pre-existing soft over-budget warning, additions are gated `ingest_manifest`-only). Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking — no brief consumption (provenance N/A); grounding gate satisfied (input is the manifest path + optional scope); autonomy scoped (checkpoint is a decision-autonomy stop, frame_scope is explicit user input, default is non-destructive deliver-what's-built); worktree-safe (manifest force-added so a fresh worktree resumes). **Sync owed (13/13).** Follow-up: design-ingest's review-pause handoff still says "run it straight off this manifest" (one-shot framing) — update it to mention auto-checkpoint/resume so the two halves agree.

### 2026-06-17 — context-budget linter: deterministic pre-commit enforcement (`b4473429`)

**What.** New `tools/validate-context-budget.js`, wired into `npm test` (which `.husky/pre-commit` runs) + `quality`. **Why.** The owner asked whether the budget/step-3b discipline can actually be *enforced* — a hook/method — rather than living as instructions an agent can skip. **Finding (the corpus tension).** A hard gate at the real per-step budget (~300 lines) is impossible without a corpus refactor: existing steps routinely run 400–975 lines (design-synthesize step-06 = 975, ~18k tokens), and the 5 active sessions write in that style — gating it would block everyone. **Scope.** So the linter splits enforcement: HARD (exit 1, near-zero false-positive) on a RUNAWAY CEILING (any step over 1100 lines / 95KB — above the current max, catches a future mega-step, grandfathers nothing) and a STEP-3B GATE-GUARD (create-workflow must keep `step-03b-review.md` and route to it — the adversarial gate cannot be silently deleted); WARN only on real-budget (350 lines / 28KB) + must-do density (proxy), with `--strict` to escalate once the corpus is shrunk. Judgment checks (buried constraint, one-job, did-the-review-actually-run) are NOT mechanically enforceable and stay in step-03b as the adversarial instruction. Verified the full corpus passes (exit 0, 21 soft warnings) and proved both hard checks bite (`--strict` → exit 1; simulated step-3b removal → fail) BEFORE wiring, so the gate doesn't break active sessions. **Delivery.** Fork-internal (`tools/` + `package.json`), so NOT synced — the fork commit boundary is the complete authoring locus. Pushed `myfork/custom` → `b4473429`; full `npm test` green (caught + fixed eslint `unicorn/explicit-length-check` + prettier on the new file before commit).

### 2026-06-17 — create-workflow: mandatory adversarial review gate before wiring (`5614e548`)

**What.** New `create-workflow/steps/step-03b-review.md` — a mandatory adversarial review gate inserted between build (step-03) and wire (step-04). **Why.** The owner asked whether the context-budget work was actually *proactive* — i.e. when an agent builds a workflow, does it adversarially review the result? It did not: create-workflow built and wired with only a self-verify checklist (the building agent ticking its own box), never an adversarial pass and never against the durable principles; the expert skill's Mode-1 review was reactive (loads only when triggered). **Scope.** step-03b re-reads every built file from disk under an explicit adversarial posture ("break it, don't confirm it"), attacks each step against the durable principles — context budget (overdense-step / inlined-corpus / buried-constraint / undelegated-read), grounding, autonomy scoping, provenance, greenfield-in-brownfield, structural — classifies Blocking/Concerns/Nits, fixes every Blocking finding and re-reviews until clean (≤3 loops, else flags for manual redesign), then wires. **Prefers an isolated sub-agent** for the review (independent context + practices the very context-budget discipline this enforces) with a same-context fresh re-read as fallback — deliberately **no hard dependency on the Mason-local skill**, since create-workflow is synced to 13 targets where the skill may not exist. The skill remains the canonical rubric. step-03 repoints to 3b ("built-but-unreviewed is never wired"); workflow.md declares the gate + `{wf_review_verdict}`; step-04 surfaces the verdict in its completion report; SKILL.md Mode 2 notes the workflow now bakes the gate in. **Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom` → `5614e548`; pre-commit full test suite green; synced 13/13. This is the "make it proactive" follow-on to `e8a9cf93` below.

### 2026-06-17 — context-budget durable principle: fork expert made context-aware + decomposition checks (`e8a9cf93` + skill v1.6)

**What.** A new durable principle — **context budget & workflow decomposition** — added to the fork expert and wired into the authoring/audit meta-workflows. New skill reference `references/context-budget.md`; SKILL.md → v1.6 (v1.5 rolled to `versions/`); fork commit `e8a9cf93` touching create-workflow/step-03-build + orchestrate-workflows/step-02-health-check. **Why.** The owner observed that long / instruction-dense workflows hit "context fatigue" — the model compresses context and drops detail — and asked that the expert understand this and be able to recommend delegating sub-workflows. Three parallel research agents established the precise basis: **context rot** (length-driven monotonic decline before the window fills; Chroma 2025), **curse of instructions** (reliability slips past ~10 simultaneous constraints; IFScale), **lost-in-the-middle** (position; Liu et al.), **effective window ≈ 50–65%** of advertised (NVIDIA RULER), and the **compaction trap** (LLM self-summarization drops exact figures / edge-cases / constraint wording first — the dropped exceptions ARE the spec). The fork already does the right things by instinct (orchestrator + `steps/` = progressive disclosure; handoff artifacts = externalized state); the gap was sub-agent fan-out and an explicit budget. **Scope.** `references/context-budget.md` states the phenomenon, the budget (one job/step, ≤~10 must-dos, constraints at top + point of use, pointer-over-inline, effective-window discipline), and the decomposition decision-rule keyed on read-heavy/parallelizable (→ sub-agent fan-out returning a distilled artifact) vs. long-sequential-build (→ per-phase fresh context + durable manifest) vs. write-one-coherent-artifact (→ single-threaded), reconciling Anthropic's multi-agent research win with Cognition's *Don't Build Multi-Agents*, plus the non-negotiable handoff contract. SKILL.md weaves it into Mode 1 (Review check), Mode 2 (Author decision-rule at design time), and adds Mode-3 root-cause class `context-budget-overflow`. create-workflow/step-03-build now builds within budget (RULES + step-content guidelines + verify item + failure mode); orchestrate-workflows/step-02-health-check gains a Context Budget audit section with finding categories. **Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom` → `e8a9cf93`; pre-commit full test suite green. Skills are not synced (SKILL.md live immediately); the two fork workflow edits are **Sync owed (13/13)**. Mode 1 self-review of the fork edits: 0 blocking — no brief consumption (provenance N/A), additive checks only, house-style verify/failure-mode shape matching peer workflows.

### 2026-06-17 — design-ingest: new workflow + ingest_manifest input_kind + §2f-bis section-coverage gate (`293dfcc9`)

**What.** New `implement/design-ingest/` workflow (5 files: workflow.md, manifest-schema.md, 3 steps) + 3 design-implement patches (workflow.md input_kind/state-vars/Input-Resolution; step-01 MANIFEST PATH; step-03 §2f-bis Section-coverage gate). **Why.** Running design-implement on a ~140KB JSX bundle hit two failures: the whole bundle overflowed one ingest context (so the exhaustive per-component catalog got shortcut), and delegating ingest by feature-area with narrow prompts let a whole SECTION inside a present frame (the Reconciliation block; also SellerSmart dispatch) go unenumerated — §2f frame-coverage is frame-granular and greens out on a missing section whose inner primitives match elsewhere. Root class `contract-dimension-gap` (section axis missing beneath a present frame) plus a context-budget cause. **Scope.** design-ingest fans out one isolated agent **per frame** (fan-out unit = frame, never feature-area), enumerates each frame's complete top-level section list under a FRAME-COMPLETENESS GATE (empty list on a drawn frame → halt), and emits a durable, reviewable manifest with a pre-seeded grid scaffold (every (frame,section) = a row), then PAUSES. design-implement consumes it via `input_kind: ingest_manifest` (skips re-ingest; refusal gate on an incomplete manifest) and §2f-bis makes every section a mandatory grid row (Tier-1 when a present frame lacks one) — denominator from the gated scaffold or enumerated in-context on URL/bundle runs. Same named-gate shape as page-shell §2d and frame-coverage §2f. **Delivery.** Pushed `myfork/custom` → `293dfcc9`; pre-commit suite green; sync owed (13/13). Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking; follow-ups (e) brief-provenance propagation when source is a synthesize_bundle, (f) checklist/template section-coverage checkbox.

### 2026-06-17 — design-implement: authoritative §13-lookup denominator — the rendered Linked-records list (`4910a3e1`)

inbound-flow session: owner asked "does design-implement ledger the implementation of the link-to-record drawers? orders.html contains [6] link-to drawers that ALL need checking against Claude Design (source of truth) and the workflow often misses these," with a live supply-order detail drawer screenshot showing a "Linked records" section — `Catalog item · Route warehouse · Shipping lane · Supply source · Inbound batch · Import run`. Diagnosis (Mode 3): **root class `contract-dimension-gap`** — the §2f frame-coverage axis exists (added `56d44fc9` for exactly these Orders.html lookups) but its denominator is harvested ONLY from bundle *self-declarations* (URL.3a sources 1–4: `<script src>` comments, per-frame banners, lookup→target maps). Those are declarations — they under-enumerate, are imprecise, or describe a different conditional state than the one rendered — so a lookup the comments forgot to list never enters `{design_frame_inventory}`, and §2f cannot flag a frame it never knew existed. The proof was in the screenshot: the live drawer renders `Shipping lane`, which isn't in the workflow's own harvest example (that lists `accounting-outcome` instead) — drift the workflow had no mechanism to notice, because it never counted the parent drawer's rendered linked-record rows. Fix is **add-not-cede** (the rendered "Linked records" section is evidence step-01 already opens the detail drawer to read): URL.3a gains **source 5** — capture `{design_linked_record_rows}` (one entry per rendered RecordLink row), the AUTHORITATIVE one-row-per-lookup denominator, and reconcile every row against a `role: §13-lookup` frame in `{design_frame_inventory}` (a row with no harvested frame → re-trace, else add `LOOKUP UNDER-ENUMERATED`, never silently drop; harvested §13-lookup count ≥ rendered row count). step-03 §2f runs the reconciliation BEFORE emitting Frame-coverage rows and is **denominator-source-agnostic** (brief/URL/manifest all reconcile against the rendered list, because the list is what the surface actually renders, not what a declaration claims); an under-enumerated row becomes a routed `LOOKUP UNDER-ENUMERATED` Frame-coverage row; and a drawer "present in both" is now swept for **depth** so a thin `code — name` stub against a rich bundle drawer surfaces as `MISSING in impl` rows (`PRESENT BUT THIN` Tier-1) rather than greening on "the drawer opens." step-04 §9 Frame-coverage disclosure leads with the linked-records denominator and enumerates under-enumerated + present-but-thin; §6 summary + §2f SUCCESS METRIC + 2 FAILURE MODES; workflow.md `{design_linked_record_rows}` state var + the existing frame Critical Rule extended; checklist +2 items. Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking — add-not-cede (rendered evidence in hand), no double-counting (a thin drawer's interior gaps ride the normal grid as MISSING-component deltas; the frame carries a PRESENT-BUT-THIN flag), not over-engineering (a denominator-source correction + reuse of the existing §2 sweep, not a new subsystem). Pushed `myfork/custom` → `4910a3e1`; pre-commit full test suite green; synced 13/13. Skill → `4910a3e1`.

### 2026-06-17 — design-implement: step-02b capability delta made SYMMETRIC — catch the uplift, not just the regression (`f2d70fd1`)

inbound-flow session: owner reported design-implement is "awful right now — running blind," with two screenshots — the live prod `/orders` (a flat severity-sorted orders table with country-filter chips + a reconciliation/EOS column) vs the design handoff for Supply orders (lane-by-handler segmentation `EU→UK Bexley Direct` / `DE→UK ATS AVASK` / `US→UK Kata` / `UK domestic SellerSmart` with per-lane counts + capital, an analytics/disposition band `Blocked / Intentional / Recv gap / EOS soon / Awaiting / Clean`, a per-row disposition action column, and Ledger/Staged/PO/Blocked-stock/SellerSmart-push co-view tabs). A prior run had concluded "Design side is now fully cataloged … the picture so far points at treatment/token alignment rather than net-new capability, with no capability-regression risk since production is a structural superset" — *while the mapping agent was still resolving production file paths*. Diagnosis (Mode 3): **root class `contract-dimension-gap`**, and the precise mirror of every prior design-implement patch. step-02b (the regression preflight, `71656fb1`) is built entirely around the **DROPPED** axis (production ∖ handoff → halt for intent); §3 computed **ADDED** but labelled it "informational," and **CHANGED** as "note it; it flows through the normal grid." So a redesign whose *entire point* is a capability UPLIFT drops nothing → empty DROP set → §4 "proceeding additively, additive and replacement are equivalent, no halt" → and with no machinery foregrounding the add, the agent free-associated to the cheapest signal it had (raw-hex → canonical-token debt) and emitted a backwards *scope* verdict from *treatment* evidence. The DROP axis caught "redesign removes a capability"; nothing caught "redesign adds a capability the live page lacks" — silently ADDED-but-unbuilt, the inverse of the page-shell/frame-coverage/content-lane "silently ABSENT" misses. Fix is **add-not-cede** (both inventories are already gathered in §1/§2, so the ADD axis is verifiable from evidence in hand, like page-shell §2d): step-02b retitled *Capability-Delta Preflight*; §3 promotes ADDED + DEEPENED to first-class `{added_capabilities}`/`{deepened_capabilities}` → `{uplift_capabilities}` and narrows CHANGED to treatment-only; §4b names a non-empty uplift and sets strategy ≥ `additive` (no halt — implementing the handoff is decision autonomy, unlike a drop which is intent); §4's empty-DROP branch hands off to §4b instead of auto-concluding restyle. Two new gates: a **running-blind gate** (workflow.md Critical Rule + step-02b MANDATORY + SUCCESS/FAILURE) forbidding "restyle-only / structural superset / no-net-new" as a prior or from treatment evidence — the verdict is an OUTPUT of the §3 bidirectional delta, asserted only after both surfaces are inventoried; and a **symmetric-capability Critical Rule** in workflow.md (the ADD twin of the existing regression rule). Downstream wiring: step-03 §2h tags uplift sub-structure `capability-build` (not collapsed to "MISSING component → restyle"); step-04 §1 fix-type "Capability build" + §5 ledger rule (a `capability-build` row is `applied (built)`, never `deferred(out-of-scope)`) + a new §9 "Capabilities built (uplift)" disclosure mirroring "Capabilities removed (orphaned actions)" (every added/deepened capability confirmed built or flagged Tier-1 incomplete) + matching SUCCESS METRIC + FAILURE MODE; workflow.md +4 state vars + architecture line. Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking — DROP halt preserved, ADD path correctly does NOT halt (decision autonomy), autonomous-mode behavior stated for both directions, not over-engineering (symmetry with machinery that already exists, no soak/drift-flag). Pushed `myfork/custom` → `f2d70fd1`; pre-commit full test suite green; synced 13/13. Skill → `f2d70fd1`.

### 2026-06-17 — design-implement: transcription fidelity + render-compare done-gate (copy & chrome) (`e91bccd3`)

inbound-flow session, after shipping a cost-reconciliation drawer (PR #2279) that the owner then A/B'd against the handoff PNG: the impl read visibly worse than the design despite a green grid. Mode 3 → Mode 2. **Root cause class: `contract-dimension-gap`** — the comparison grid is component × state × property (CSS cells), so it has NO row for **copy** or for **frame chrome**; both fell through and the agent *interpreted where it was told to transcribe* (substituted a stock `SheetHeader` for the design's `‹ Back to Supply order` breadcrumbed header, relabeled "Open full cost record" → "Open full order", paraphrased "deferred import" → "import", swapped "EUR → GBP" → "€ → £"). Every CSS cell matched, so "all green" *manufactured* false confidence. The owner's framing drove the fix shape: NOT new per-string grid axes (that's scaffolding compensating for not following the existing "source is truth" rule) — instead (1) name the prohibited move in workflow.md Critical Rules ("you are TRANSCRIBING, not designing — covers copy & chrome"; the grid is necessary-NOT-sufficient; a green grid is not "done"; split "don't screenshot" into ingest-reads-values vs verification-renders-to-compare), and (2) one real check at the right altitude in step-04 **§5b**: a transcription pass (chrome + copy → verbatim OR forced-and-logged deviation, riding the *existing* ledger → existing §9 list, no new report section) + the render-compare done-gate (built surface beside the design render; owed-and-routed to `verify`/design-review when unrenderable). Add-not-cede because copy & chrome are verifiable from the design source — a static source-vs-source string diff catches them with no render (the floor; render-compare is the ideal). Matching SUCCESS METRIC + FAILURE MODE echoes; 2 checklist lines. Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking; tightens autonomy scoping (paraphrasing copy is *intent* autonomy, now explicitly unlicensed). Pushed `myfork/custom` → `e91bccd3`; pre-commit suite green; synced 13/13. Skill → `e91bccd3`.

### 2026-06-17 — design-implement: regression-surface preflight (step-02b) — handoff-vs-production capability delta + strategy gate (`71656fb1`)

inbound-flow session: owner asked for "a new way of entering" design-implement when handed a handoff URL — Claude should proactively "check what was changed from this handoff relative to production," because the new handoff "may have dropped (which of the five to restore — routing, linked records, full economics/cost-recon, dual-status header, activity timeline) relative to production," and offer the user an implementation choice (additive / partially additive / replacement-in-styling). Mode 2. The need is real and recurring: design-implement treats the handoff as the spec and would faithfully reproduce a redesign's *omissions* — and the component sweep greens out on a dropped whole-capability because its inner primitives (pills, money cells, RecordLinks) exist elsewhere in the impl. There was a post-hoc backstop (step-04 §9 "Capabilities removed (orphaned actions)") but it fires mechanically AFTER the apply on now-zero-caller actions; the owner wants it PROACTIVE, at intake, capability-level, with a strategy choice. Built a new **step-02b-regression-surface** between map (step-02) and grid (step-03): §1 inventories `{production_capabilities}` (feature-level — routing/sub-surfaces, §13 linked records, economics/cost-recon, composite status/header, activity/audit, bulk actions/filters, wired mutations — from step-02's outputs), §2 inventories `{handoff_capabilities}` (`{design_components}` + `{design_frame_inventory}` + brief §7), §3 computes `{dropped_capabilities}` (prod minus handoff). Non-empty → **HALT** with a regression report + a 4-way strategy menu (`restyle-only` keep-all · `additive` · `partial` per-capability · `replacement`), recommendation leading non-destructive. The keep/drop decision is *intent* → halt-by-default; autonomous mode defaults to restyle-only and discloses, never silently replaces (intent autonomy out of scope). `{capability_dispositions}` then constrains downstream: step-03 §2h flags `capability-protected` rows (a grid row that would delete a kept capability), step-04 disposes them `deferred(capability-protected)` and the §9 report states the chosen strategy + every kept/dropped capability, with dropped capabilities routed to the existing §9 orphaned-action confirmation. Guards: skips cleanly on a brand-new surface (no production to regress); does NOT mis-score an undrawn-but-promised frame (brief §7 / `{design_frame_inventory}`) as dropped — ties into the recent §2f frame-coverage work to avoid a false-positive against itself. step-01 now announces the check up front (the proactive "I'm going to check what changed vs production" UX the owner described). Wiring: step-02 COMPLETION → step-02b → step-03; workflow.md +5 state vars + a Critical Rule + the sequence line; checklist Step-2b section. Mode 1 self-review: 0 blocking. Pushed `myfork/custom` → `71656fb1`; pre-push suite green; synced 13/13. Skill bumped → `71656fb1`. **Refinement (`80ad60da`, same session):** owner asked the gate "lean into advisory mode, as opposed to 'can the user list this'." §4 reworked from a bare strategy menu (whose `partial` made the user assemble the keep/drop list) to an **advisory**: a per-capability KEEP / SAFE-TO-DROP verdict with grounded reasons + one recommended plan presented as a decision to approve-or-adjust ("I'll apply this unless you'd rather change it"). §5 records `partial` as the AI-proposed advised mix (AI-authored, user-confirmed); new FAILURE MODE bans offloading the keep/drop analysis to the user; step-01 announce + workflow.md Critical Rule aligned. Validated by a simulated live run on the user's `Supply Order Detail - Blocked-stock build` handoff — production drawer (`supply-orders-drawer.tsx`, 1998 lines) inventoried 11 capability groups (dual-status header · §15 economics/cost-recon · 8 §13 linked-record drawers · activity timeline · routing + §7 raw-exchange audit · …); the owner's anticipated dropped-five mapped cleanly to the report. (WebFetch couldn't pull the >10MB bundle — real step-01 uses curl; the sim grounded the handoff side on the frame name + owner's stated scope.) Synced 13/13. Skill → `80ad60da`.

### 2026-06-17 — design-implement: token-provenance disclose-and-cede (per-screen vs canonical tokens) (`b4ad51cb`)

inbound-flow session: owner pasted a live `design-impl` run (cost-recon worktree) that asserted "Tokens map ~1:1," hit a counterexample (`--status-success-text` / `--status-warning-text` / `--subtle-foreground` absent from `tokens.css`), then *resolved* it by finding `--status-*-text` defined in `supply-orders.css` lines 34–50 and concluding "the bundle was generated from this exact CSS, so the mapping is effectively 1:1." So the fix is **disclose-and-cede, never gate**, a sibling of the content-lane (§2c) and policy-conformance (§2e) cedes. Root class `contract-dimension-gap`: the token comparison was missing the *provenance* axis (it checked existence, not which layer it resolved from). step-02 §5 now resolves the value AND tags `{impl_token_provenance}` (`scope: canonical|per-screen` against `docs/design-policy.md` §8's `tokens.css`+`@theme` surface; `semantic_class: shared-semantic|local-constant`) + "resolve every token before any 1:1 claim" (the snippet's literal bug — `--subtle-foreground` was folded into 1:1 while still unresolved); step-03 §2g emits one `NON-CANONICAL TOKEN` disclosure row per shared-semantic per-screen token (not a delta, not in `{delta_count}`, not gated; `local-constant` per-screen tokens NOT flagged to avoid disclosure spam); step-04 §9 gains a mandatory "Token provenance (non-canonical)" section + SUCCESS/FAILURE rows; checklist + workflow.md state-var registry (`{token_noncanonical_count}`).
